## Look pack chosen

Alpine timber chalet. Black walnut, charcoal soapstone, honey pine, rust red and cream. Distinct from v2-v8 in every slot Daniel named.

| slot | v9 |
|---|---|
| sleeve | chunky oatmeal cream cable-knit |
| C02 table | dark black walnut plank, deep chocolate-brown, bold flowing open grain with darker streaks, dead-matte hardwax oil, thick square edge |
| C02 background | wide whitewashed pine board walls, grey fieldstone fireplace with a hand-hewn pine beam mantel, cream knitted stockings, red gingham bows and cream painted wooden ornaments on the tree |
| C03/C04 counter | honed charcoal soapstone, soft pale veining, completely matte |
| C03/C04 kitchen | alpine chalet kitchen - LEFT in C03, RIGHT in C04 |
| C05 F1 door | vertical plank-and-batten door in warm honey-stained pine, six wide boards with V-grooves, three horizontal battens |
| knitwear | rust-red and cream nordic with a row of cream snowflakes across the yoke, charcoal knitted lounge trousers, thick oatmeal socks |
| C06 child | girl, about 7, shoulder-length wavy auburn hair |
| C07 children | girl about 9 with light blonde hair in a high ponytail, boy about 6 with curly light brown hair |
| C05 F2 + C07 room | double-height great room, flat coffered grid 3 bays across x 5 deep, deep white crown cornice, black steel glazed window wall |
| C06 room | bedroom under a shallow barrel vault of white tongue-and-groove boards springing off a flat plaster border |

## The projection - rebuilt from Daniel's four reference screenshots

This is the largest change in the round and the one most worth his eye. Daniel attached four screenshots of real projector output and said the animation on the walls and ceiling must be "even more impressive and wow" and must look like them. The projection paragraph was therefore **not** carried over from v8. It was rewritten from what is actually in those four pictures, in this order:

1. a snow-laden pine forest running along the bottom of the wall
2. an ornate gilded fairy-tale Christmas city of spires and onion domes on the left, every window lit gold
3. a lit steam train of eight gold-windowed carriages crossing on the right
4. wide aurora ribbons in emerald green, rose magenta, warm gold and soft teal
5. one colossal spiral whirlpool of seven concentric rings drawing in to a white-gold core
6. Santa's sleigh and eight reindeer sweeping out of the spiral's mouth on a trail of gold
7. a pale blue cratered moon with a tiny second sleigh crossing its face
8. floating wrapped gifts at five or six depths
9. a dense star field with shooting stars, large six-point snowflakes, glowing cloud wisps
10. a heavy fall of tiny light points over the whole surface

### Two standing bans were deliberately lifted

Both of these ran through v1-v8 and both are **gone in v9 and v10**, on purpose, because Daniel's own references break them:

| ban that was lifted | why |
|---|---|
| `NO cold colours - no cyan, no teal, no electric blue, no violet, no grey` | his references are emphatically multicoloured - emerald, magenta, teal and a pale blue moon are the loudest things in all four pictures |
| `must NOT look like ... a spiral vortex` | the spiral whirlpool is the centrepiece of two of the four references |

The anti-sci-fi guard rail was **kept**, reworded so it still fires: the spiral must read as *an AURORA WHIRLPOOL made of Christmas light with a sleigh flying out of its mouth, not a space nebula*.

If Daniel wants the old warm-gold-only look back, that is one line to restore - say so and it goes back.

### The ceiling was made structurally honest

He asked that "the ceiling of the rooms it's looking real in terms of shape and it makes sense". Every B-roll 2 prompt now carries a dedicated `HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE CEILING` block (or `... ON THE SURFACES` for C07) that ties the projection to the actual built geometry of that room: which timbers it crosses, where it has to break and step, where it distorts across an angled reveal or a curved vault, and what it reads as from the floor. The projection can no longer float free of the ceiling it is supposedly landing on.

## Settings

`gpt_image_2_5 - variant sunburst - quality medium - resolution 2k - aspect_ratio 9:16 - count 1 - use_unlim false` - output 1520 x 2688. Workspace Oneofone1.

## C01 - The Christmas store end-cap

*Section:* Hook
*Status:* REBUILT ON THE RIGHT SOURCE - g3/g4 GENERATED FOR REVIEW
*Defect note:* this C01 section was written on 2026-09-20 but never landed in this file - it was authored as a fresh `prompts.md` while this archive already existed at 67 KB with C02-C07, so the write did not merge. Recovered and inserted here.
*What it is:* the v7 C01 store shot again, with the store properly dressed for Christmas and the camera angle corrected.

- frames: `v9_c01_g1.png`, `v9_c01_g2.png` (built on a misread source - see below), `v9_c01_g3.png`, `v9_c01_g4.png` (the real brief)
- clips: none yet

*Reused from* v7 C01 in spirit, **rewritten as text** - changed: the store is now dressed for Christmas (garland with fairy lights and baubles along the shelf top, red velvet bows on the shelf edges, a decorated tree down the aisle, hanging baubles and a seasonal sign over the aisle), and the camera angle is corrected. The carton paragraph, the banner paragraph, the woman paragraph, the wordmark spelling clause, the colour block and the quality tail are carried from v7 C01.

*What was "a little bit weird" about v7 C01, and the fix:* three things, all inherited from the LED reference frame - the camera sat too close behind her, her head was cropped hard at the left edge, and the gondola skewed away at a sharp oblique so the shelves raked across the frame. Both options here step the camera back and put her whole upper body inside the frame. Option A squares the shelving up to the camera so the shelves run parallel to the bottom edge. Option B replaces the oblique with a clean one-point perspective down the aisle.

**References attached**

| slot | file | why |
|---|---|---|
| @Image 1 | `references/v9_c01_r1.png` | owns the carton artwork |

**Prompt - option A** (`v9_c01_g1.png`) - `gpt_image_2_5 - sunburst - 9:16 - 2k - medium` - job `5a8a4a66-4473-404e-9fe7-075132ba9651`

Camera level and stepped back, shelving square-on with the shelves parallel to the frame bottom, her at the left third uncropped, yellow basket.

```text
Extremely high quality candid vertical iPhone photo inside a big bright Scandinavian-style warehouse furniture store decorated for Christmas, taken from a couple of steps behind a woman who is facing a full end-cap display of our product.

FRAMING AND ANGLE - THIS IS THE IMPORTANT PART: the camera is at normal eye level, held straight and level, standing a comfortable few steps back so the whole display is seen square-on and the aisle has room to breathe. The woman stands at the LEFT THIRD of the frame, seen from behind at three-quarters, her head and shoulders well inside the frame and not cropped by the edge, a yellow plastic shopping basket hanging from her right hand low at her side. The white retail shelving unit stands SQUARE-ON to the camera across the centre and right of the frame, its shelves running straight and level and parallel to the bottom of the picture, not skewed away at a sharp angle. Clean, symmetrical, easy to read. Polished pale concrete floor, the wide aisle continuing away behind the display, other decorated store aisles softly visible in the distance.

THE PRODUCT WALL: four full shelves stacked end to end with OUR carton, exactly as it appears in the attached reference: a tall upright rectangular tuck-top carton, square footprint, proportions about 1 to 1.6, forest green body, a dark red band across the top carrying a gold THEXMASGLOW wordmark, a cream serif headline CHRISTMAS LIGHT PROJECTOR under it, a gold star-and-tree burst over a printed render of a smooth white dome-topped cylindrical projector standing on a separate white four-legged splayed stand base, a slim red remote printed beside it, a gold village skyline along the bottom edge, a thin gold foil border, and no transparent window anywhere. The cartons stand upright side by side, front panels facing the camera, identical to each other, perfectly faced in neat rows, dozens of them. A row of the same cartons is stacked on top of the unit as well. At the right edge of the display stands a tall narrow vertical forest green printed banner with a gold border, a gold star, the gold wordmark THEXMASGLOW, the cream serif headline CHRISTMAS LIGHT PROJECTOR, a large photograph of the white dome projector throwing warm gold trees and snowflakes across a dark living-room wall, and the lines 16 Patterns, Remote Control, Timer down the lower half.

THE CHRISTMAS DRESSING: the store is properly decorated for Christmas. A thick green garland with warm white fairy lights and red and gold baubles runs along the top edge of the shelving unit, small red velvet bows are clipped to the shelf edges, a decorated Christmas tree with warm white lights stands further down the aisle, more garland and a few oversized red and gold baubles hang from the exposed warehouse ceiling above the aisle, and a red and cream seasonal hanging sign floats over the aisle in the distance. Festive but still unmistakably a real store, not a set.

THE WOMAN: late 20s, long dark brown hair falling loose down her back, an oversized oatmeal cream knitted sweater, and matching American-flag Christmas pyjama bottoms - soft brushed cotton pyjama trousers with bold red and white horizontal stripes and small white five-pointed stars printed across the navy blue waistband panel, slightly loose the way real pyjamas fit - and white ribbed socks in white trainers. She is standing still, looking up at the display, one hand down holding the yellow basket.

The wordmark printed on the cartons and on the banner must read exactly THEXMASGLOW - eleven letters, T-H-E-X-M-A-S-G-L-O-W, correctly spelled, sharp and legible, with no invented or misspelled brand name anywhere in the picture.

COLOUR: neutral white balance, the way an iPhone auto-corrects a bright store. The picture is NOT orange, NOT amber, NOT sepia and there is no warm colour cast laid over the whole frame. The shelving reads as clean white, the floor as pale grey concrete, her sweater as true oatmeal cream, the cartons as true forest green and gold.

LIGHTING AND QUALITY: bright, clean, evenly lit store interior, plenty of light on the display and on her, no dark corners, no heavy shadows. Shot on an iPhone 16 Pro and straight out of the camera roll - an ordinary everyday snapshot, not a magazine photo and not a rendered image. Extremely high quality, tack sharp and in focus everywhere, crisp legible printing on every carton, realistic fabric and skin texture. Absolutely nothing blurry: no motion blur, no soft focus, no background blur, no bokeh, no grain, no film look, no cinematic grade, no vignette. Normal wide-angle lens, vertical lines vertical and parallel, no fisheye distortion.

FRAMING: leave a clean band of plain store ceiling across the very top of the image - roughly the top eighth of the frame - free of boxes, garland, banners, hanging signs and lettering, so a caption can sit there. Do not pull the camera back or shrink the woman to make that room.

No captions, no watermarks, no added lettering anywhere in the image other than the printing on our own cartons and banner.
```

*Difference between the two:* A is the clean product wall - shelves dead level, the whole unit readable, the banner full height, the least "weird" of the three store takes. B sells the store harder - the aisle runs away into depth, the ceiling decorations carry the eye, and she is walking rather than standing, which gives a video prompt something to continue.

### The misread - g1 and g2 are built on the wrong shot

The brief said *"in the style of C1 from video 7"*. That was read as **this product's** v7 C01, the
Christmas store end-cap, so g1 and g2 are two more store end-caps. Daniel corrected it:

> *"I ment in the style of C1 from video 7 from the led curtain"*

The real source is `MERRYGLOWUSA - LED CURTAIN LIGHTS/content/video_7_assets/starting_frames/v7_c01_g1.png`
- the IKEA-style **pallet reveal**: a tall pallet of cartons under a matte black cloth, one uniformed
member of staff holding each bottom corner, a crowd of shoppers watching, the instant before the
cover comes off. Nothing to do with an end-cap. g1 and g2 stay in this archive as the record of the
misread; they are not candidates.

### Revision round - 2026-09-20

g3 and g4 are written off the LED frame's **staging**, not its camera. Three things made the LED
original read oddly, and all three are fixed in the prompt text rather than left to the reference:

1. The cover read as a smooth cone / draped sculpture. Both takes now spell out a **rectangular
   stepped stack** on a pallet - a big block with a smaller block stepped back on top - so hard box
   corners and straight vertical edges show through the cloth.
2. The crowd ate the bottom third as huge foreground silhouettes. Shoppers are now explicitly small
   and confined to the **bottom fifth**, never blocking the stack or the staff.
3. The LED frame is dead symmetrical with the vanishing point in the middle. Both takes are pushed
   **off axis** - g3 three-quarter from the right at chest height, g4 raised above head height and
   off to the left.

The store is dressed hard for Christmas, which is the one thing that carries forward from the g1/g2
direction.

*Reused from* the LED curtain v7 C01 for staging only - changed: the product is the projector
carton, the store is dressed for Christmas, the stack is stated as rectangular, the crowd is pushed
down and back, and the camera is taken off the centre line.

**Prompt - g3** (`v9_c01_g3.png`) - `gpt_image_2_5 - sunburst - 9:16 - 2k - medium` - job `20a84084-564a-4be5-ba95-3513cc5f2de4`

Three-quarter from the right at chest height, aisle running away to the upper left, two faces of the
stack lit differently, stack just left of centre.

```text
Extremely high quality candid vertical iPhone photo inside a big Christmas-decorated homeware superstore: two uniformed staff stand either side of a tall pallet of cardboard cartons completely covered by a smooth matte black cloth, a small crowd of shoppers watching from the foreground, the instant before the cover comes off.

Use the attached reference photograph ONLY for the staging - the covered stack standing tall in the aisle with one member of staff at each side holding a bottom corner of the black cloth and shoppers watching from the near side. Do NOT copy its camera position.

FRAMING AND ANGLE - THIS IS THE CHANGE: shoot it from THREE QUARTERS, not square on. The camera stands off to the right of the aisle centre line at chest height, so the aisle runs away diagonally to the upper left of the frame and the covered stack is seen from its front-right corner - two faces of the stack visible at once, one catching more light than the other, giving it real depth and real weight. The vanishing point is NOT in the middle of the frame and the picture is NOT symmetrical. The covered stack stands just left of the centre of the frame, its top reaching well above the shoppers' heads into the upper third of the picture.

THE CLOTH AND WHAT IS UNDER IT: the cloth is draped over an obviously RECTANGULAR stepped stack of cardboard cartons on a low wooden pallet - a big block at the bottom, a smaller block stepped back on top of it - so hard box corners, straight vertical edges and the flat steps of the stack read clearly through the fabric. It must NOT look like a smooth cone, a tent or a draped sculpture: it looks exactly like a pallet of boxes with a sheet thrown over it. The cartons themselves are COMPLETELY HIDDEN - not one box face, logo or printed word is visible anywhere, the attached product-box photo is only a guide to the real-world size and proportion of the cartons hiding under the cloth. The cloth reaches the floor on all sides and puddles slightly on the tiles.

THE CHRISTMAS STORE: a big bright modern warehouse-style homeware store dressed hard for Christmas - tall steel shelving racks running away down the aisle on both sides packed with boxed homeware, heavy green garland with red bows and gold baubles swagged along the top rail of every shelf run, oversized red and gold baubles hanging from the exposed ceiling structure, two decorated Christmas trees with warm white lights standing further down the aisle, red and white candy-stripe signage on the shelf ends, a large glowing blue departmental sign high up in the distance, exposed ceiling with bright strip lights, polished pale grey floor tiles.

THE STAFF: two store employees, one on each side at the base of the covered stack, both in the store's uniform of a bright yellow polo shirt and dark navy trousers with a lanyard, each holding a bottom corner of the black cloth in both hands, braced and looking at each other, clearly about to pull the cloth off together. They are not smiling at the camera.

THE SHOPPERS: a small gathered crowd of ordinary shoppers watching, seen from behind and in three-quarter view at the bottom of the frame - winter coats, scarves, one pushing a trolley. They are SMALL in the frame and low: their heads and shoulders occupy only the bottom fifth of the picture and none of them is silhouetted huge in the foreground. The covered stack and the two staff are never blocked by a shopper's head.

COLOUR: neutral white balance, the way an iPhone auto-corrects a bright shop. The picture is NOT orange, NOT amber, NOT sepia and there is no warm colour cast laid over the whole frame. The cloth reads as true matte black, the floor as clean pale grey, the staff polos as true bright yellow, the garland as natural green with true red bows.

LIGHTING AND QUALITY: bright, clean and evenly lit under the store's overhead strip lights, no dark corners, no heavy shadows, no blown highlights. Shot on an iPhone and straight out of the camera roll - an ordinary everyday snapshot of something happening in a shop, not a magazine photo and not a rendered image. Extremely high quality, tack sharp and in focus everywhere from the shoppers in front to the sign in the distance, crisp detail in the weave of the black cloth, the cardboard edges under it, the garland and the shelf labels, realistic skin and fabric texture. Absolutely nothing blurry: no motion blur, no soft focus, no background blur, no bokeh, no grain, no film look, no cinematic grade, no vignette. Normal wide-angle lens, vertical lines vertical and parallel, no fisheye distortion.

FRAMING: leave a clean band of plain ceiling across the very top of the image - roughly the top eighth of the frame - free of the covered stack, of hanging baubles, of signage and of any other object, so a caption can sit there. Do not pull the camera back or shrink the covered stack to make that room.

No captions, no watermarks, no lettering on the cloth, no user-interface elements and no social-media overlays anywhere in the image.
```

The covered stack and the two staff are never blocked by a shopper's head.

COLOUR: neutral white balance, the way an iPhone auto-corrects a bright shop. The picture is NOT orange, NOT amber, NOT sepia and there is no warm colour cast laid over the whole frame. The cloth reads as true matte black, the floor as clean pale grey, the staff polos as true bright yellow, the garland as natural green with true red bows.

LIGHTING AND QUALITY: bright, clean and evenly lit under the store's overhead strip lights, no dark corners, no heavy shadows, no blown highlights. Shot on an iPhone and straight out of the camera roll - an ordinary everyday snapshot of something happening in a shop, not a magazine photo and not a rendered image. Extremely high quality, tack sharp and in focus everywhere from the shoppers in front to the sign in the distance, crisp detail in the weave of the black cloth, the cardboard edges under it, the garland and the shelf labels, realistic skin and fabric texture. Absolutely nothing blurry: no motion blur, no soft focus, no background blur, no bokeh, no grain, no film look, no cinematic grade, no vignette. Normal wide-angle lens, vertical lines vertical and parallel, no fisheye distortion.

FRAMING: leave a clean band of plain ceiling across the very top of the image - roughly the top eighth of the frame - free of the covered stack, of hanging baubles, of signage and of any other object, so a caption can sit there. Do not pull the camera back or shrink the covered stack to make that room.

No captions, no watermarks, no lettering on the cloth, no user-interface elements and no social-media overlays anywhere in the image.
```

*Difference between g3 and g4:* g3 is at shopper eye level, so the stack towers and the reveal has
more threat to it. g4 is the phone-held-over-the-crowd angle - it reads more like something filmed by
a bystander and it keeps the crowd from ever competing with the stack, but the stack loses height.

```
Single continuous handheld iPhone shot, 4 seconds, vertical 9:16, filmed from inside the crowd over the heads of the shoppers in the foreground — their heads and shoulders stay in the bottom of the frame the whole clip. No cuts, no zoom, no camera movement beyond natural handheld sway, framing locked as in the start frame.

Start frame: two store employees in their yellow polo shirts and dark trousers stand on either side of a tall pyramid completely covered by a smooth black cloth, in a big home store aisle hung with Christmas garland, a crowd of shoppers watching from the foreground.

ACTION TIMELINE:
0.0–2.4s — The two employees grab the top corners of the black cloth, grinning, full showman energy, and one of them calls out loud and clear, spacing the numbers out with real suspense: "Three... two... one... let's see how it comes out!" On each number the crowd reacts more — heads bobbing, hands and phones rising above the foreground heads, people leaning side to side to see, the crowd chanting the numbers along with him — and on the last word everyone freezes leaning forward for a tiny breathless beat. The cloth stays fully covering the pyramid until the sentence ends — the pyramid stays a solid, rigid, unchanging shape underneath the whole time.
2.4–3.3s — Exactly on "comes out", both employees whip the cloth UP and away together in one big overhead sweep — the fabric billowing into the air with natural physics before they carry it aside — revealing a neat pyramid of identical stacked product boxes: the green THEXMASGLOW CHRISTMAS LIGHT PROJECTOR box from the attached reference image, front faces out, logos sharp and readable, every box the same real retail size.
3.3–4.0s — The crowd erupts: cheering, clapping above the heads, phones snapping photos, the person filming whooping. The employees step aside proudly, arms spread toward the pyramid. End frame: the box pyramid fully visible, crisp and readable.

AUDIO: the employee's real voice, lip-synced, matching his appearance — "Three... two... one... let's see how it comes out!" — the crowd chanting the countdown along with him, the tiny held-breath pause, then a big crowd cheer and applause after the reveal, fabric swish, live store ambience underneath. NO music, no narration, no sound effects beyond these.

REALISM — HARD RULES: the two employees keep exactly the same faces, yellow polo shirts and positions as the start frame. The revealed boxes are pixel-faithful to the attached reference box — same artwork, logo, colors, spelling, text rigid and never warping. The pyramid that is revealed is the same solid object that was under the cloth from frame one — same size, same silhouette. The store background stays identical. Real phone-camera look, slight sensor noise, no cinematic bokeh, no text overlays, no watermark.
```

Three things written as three sentences become three events.

The countdown is now written as ONE fused instant repeated three times rather than as parallel descriptions: two governing rules stated up front (the dip *is* the number, on the same frame, not a reaction that follows it; and all voices open their mouths on the same frame with nobody leading or echoing), then MOMENT ONE / MOMENT TWO / MOMENT THREE at 0.3s / 1.4s / 2.5s, each one a single event containing the unison shout and the dip together. The silent gaps are given explicit time ranges (0.6-1.4s, 1.7-2.5s) and stated as stillness as well as silence, so nothing drifts into them. The payoff line at 3.0s is explicitly marked as one employee ALONE with no crowd voice, so the unison rule does not leak onto it. Then at 3.0s one employee ALONE, with no crowd voice at all, calls out "let's see how it comes out!", and everyone freezes leaning forward for a tiny breathless beat. First, it is night: the glazed window wall shows a dark blue snow-covered night outside, there is no daylight anywhere in the room, every recessed downlight and both pendants in the house are switched off and dark, and the projection is the only light. Second, the room is decorated for Christmas: a tall real Christmas tree standing against the glazed window wall beside the fireplace with warm white lights and rust-red and antique gold ornaments and wrapped gifts beneath it, a thick evergreen garland with pine cones and rust-red velvet ribbon laid along the plaster mantel, cream knitted stockings hanging from that mantel, and a large evergreen wreath with a rust-red velvet bow hung on the glazed wall. CHANGE 2 - THE PROJECTION, and this is the part that matters most. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a deep snow-laden pine forest running the whole width of the room along the very bottom of the projection, rank behind rank of little glowing firs in emerald green and warm gold with snow heaped on every branch, and rising straight out of that forest on the left a whole ornate fairy-tale Christmas city built of lit gold - tier upon tier of slender spires, ribbed onion domes, arched galleries and pinnacled towers stacked up the wall, every arched window lit warm gold, fine gold filigree running along every cornice and balustrade, thick snow lying on every ledge and roof and dome, the whole city glowing as though it were carved out of light; and winding out of the pines on the right a long lit steam train, a dark locomotive leading eight carriages whose windows all glow warm gold, a bright plume of steam and sparks trailing back over the carriage roofs and a glowing gold rail curving away behind it through the trees; ABOVE THE FOREST AND THE CITY, wide soft aurora ribbons in emerald green, rose magenta, warm gold and soft teal sweeping up the walls in long slow curves and carrying over onto the flat coffered ceiling, five or six broad overlapping bands layered over each other so there is very little bare white plaster coffer left between them, each band shot through with a fine glittering grain so it reads as a river of stars rather than a painted stripe, where they lie flat across the coffered grid and run with the long direction of the room instead of hanging as vertical curtains, and visibly step and break at every white beam as they cross from one coffer into the next; ACROSS THE COFFERED CEILING OVERHEAD, one colossal spiral whirlpool of light filling most of the coffered field - seven concentric glowing rings of emerald green, warm gold and rose magenta winding in on each other in a slow tightening spiral and drawing down to one brilliant white-gold core at the very centre, every ring built of countless tiny points of light so the whole thing reads as a whirlpool of stars rather than a painted disc; Santa's sleigh drawn by a team of eight flying reindeer sweeping straight out of the mouth of that spiral, seen side-on in glowing warm gold and large enough to read every antler, the harness and bells on each reindeer, the curved runners and Santa himself sitting up in the sleigh with his sack behind him, a long bright tapering trail of golden light streaming back behind the runners into the spiral, the whole team lying flat across the coffers so it is keystoned and stretched by the ceiling's angle, foreshortening as it recedes across the room and broken into sections by the white beams it passes under; a big pale blue full moon glowing high on the greige plaster wall high on one side of the room, cool silver-blue and softly cratered and the one cool-coloured thing in the whole picture, with the tiny black silhouette of a second sleigh and its reindeer team crossing right over the face of it; floating wrapped Christmas gifts drifting through the whole scene at five or six different depths and sizes, each a solid glowing box in gold, emerald or rose with a bright ribbon cross and a big soft bow on top, the nearest big enough to read the weave of the ribbon, all of them turning slowly end over end as they drift; a dense field of stars at many different sizes, hundreds of them, from tiny pinpricks to a few large bright ones with soft radiating rays, with four or five long-tailed shooting stars crossing at different angles with bright tapering trails; large soft glowing six-point snowflakes in cream and pale gold at several different sizes, the nearest big enough to read every arm, drifting across the coffered panels and both side walls; faint wisps of glowing cloud between the aurora bands; and over all of it a heavy fall of hundreds of tiny bright points of light drifting down the walls like real snow caught in a beam. Even packed full, the ceiling must still read unmistakably as a real flat coffered ceiling with a regular grid of white beams and deep square coffers, never as a flat backdrop or a printed panel. THE STEAM. The long lit steam train running out of the pines pours steam like a real locomotive working on a cold still evening: it boils out of the funnel, leans back over the carriage roofs, climbs and widens and thins away, and the next plume is already forming behind it, with gold sparks lifting off it the whole time.

THE AURORA. The crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbons ripple like a long silk curtain hung in a draught - a slow swell travels the length of each ribbon, brightening it as it passes and combing its lower fringe into new folds. The arc stays where it is on the drywall; the swell inside it travels, and the ribbons that feed into the mouth of the great spiral keep streaming inward along their whole length.

THE SLEIGH. Santa's sleigh and its eight reindeer are FLYING ACROSS THE CEILING the way a plane crosses the sky, sweeping out of the mouth of the great spiral and away across the slopes. Legs gallop, heads nod, the sleigh rocks, the long trail of light streams off the runners back into the spiral, the team rises and dips. They start near one side of the vault and finish far across it. Never parked.

THE GREAT SPIRAL. The colossal whirlpool of light filling the vault is WINDING: all seven concentric rings turn steadily about the brilliant white-gold core on the ridge beam, each ring creeping inward and tightening as it goes, the countless tiny points of light inside them streaming along the curve like a slow river of stars circling a drain. The spiral never sits still and its outer boundary never slides off the ceiling.

THE FLOATING GIFTS. Each glowing wrapped gift DRIFTS through the scene at its own depth and its own speed, and every one of them is turning slowly end over end as it goes, so the ribbon cross and the big soft bow swing round into view and away again.

THE SNOW. The big soft six-point flakes and the tiny points of light fall the way heavy snow falls past a window at night - down and slightly sideways, each turning on its own centre. One fades out at the floor line as a fresh one fades in high near the ridge, so the density never thins.

Everything else travels too: gold sparks lifting off the locomotive, shooting stars streaking and fading, the lit gold windows of the fairy-tale city and of the train's carriages flickering warm, wisps of crimson and gold cloud drifting between the aurora bands, and the tiny black silhouette of the second sleigh crawling on across the face of the moon. Only the pale blue moon holds position; it simply breathes brighter and dimmer.

ONE PROJECTOR, ONE PICTURE: whatever the wall is doing, the vault is doing at the same instant and the same speed. The picture's outer boundary is anchored - it never rotates, spins or slides across the room as one sheet; only the things inside it travel. It is flat light lying on the surfaces: it skews with their slope, bends over the rafters and breaks where wall meets vault. Nothing ever leaves a surface, and nothing is projected on the floor, rug, sofa, table, tree or gifts.

THE VAULT IS FULL. The ceiling carries as much moving picture as the walls do, everywhere it appears in frame and above all in the closing seconds: the winding spiral, the aurora, the flying sleigh, drifting gifts, pouring snow and twinkling stars run right up to the ridge and into both corners. No bare un-animated patch of warm-white drywall anywhere in the closing framing.

0.8s to the end - ONE SINGLE UNBROKEN CAMERA MOVE, exactly like a person walking into a room with their chin lifting the whole way. **Diagnosis of g2 before this take** (frames pulled from `clips/v9_c05_g2.mp4`, not guessed). Three findings. **(1) The door beat overran.** The prompt gave it 0.0-0.6s; the hand is still on the brass knob at 1.6s, so roughly a third of a 5s clip went on a door push and the room only got about three seconds. **(2) The children start from a standstill.** At 2.0s and 2.5s - the first frames the room is visible - the boy is planted front-on to the lens with his arms at his sides and the girl is stationary behind him. Neither begins running until about 3.5s. That is exactly Daniel's note: *when the door opens they should already be moving*, and the front-on, staring, motionless boy is what reads as *the kids look weird*. **(3) The projection is a locked texture.** Ceiling crops at 3.2 / 3.6 / 4.0 / 4.4s put the sleigh and the aurora arcs in identical positions relative to the walnut rafters - the picture changes only because the camera changes. Same action load as g1 (8s), three fewer seconds, and the projection is what the model dropped.

**Lineage** - Reused from `v9_c05_g2` - changed: four things, all aimed at Daniel's three notes and at buying back the seconds finding (3) says the projection needs. **(a) Door beat hard-capped.** `0.0-0.6s` becomes `0.0-0.4s` with an explicit close - the door is wide open and the whole room visible by 0.4s, and the hand and door are gone from frame from 0.4s on. The walk beat starts at 0.4s and CAMERA's device line moves with it. BANNED gains: a door still opening after 0.4s, a hand still on the knob after 0.4s, a slow or drawn-out door reveal. **(b) The boy's sofa launch is removed.** He was the most expensive element in the clip - run, launch, land on his knees, bounce until the last frame - and it is also what forced him front-on to the lens first. He now stays back-to-camera and simply keeps running past the sectional and deeper into the room toward the tree, still running on the last frame. BANNED gains: a child facing the lens front-on; a child climbing, jumping or landing on the sofa. POSITIVE LOCKS updated to match. The girl's run and point are untouched. **(c) The duplicate motion list deleted.** The walk beat ended with *All through it the steam pours off the train, the aurora ripples...* - a verbatim restatement of THE SIX MOVING THINGS, which seedance_2_5_prompting_guide.md says degrades rather than reinforces. Said once now, at the top. **(d) An anatomy lock added** under EXACTLY TWO CHILDREN - normal child proportions, correct limbs and joints, five fingers, faces that never smear or change - with the matching bans, against the *look weird* note. Everything else - the six moving things, ONE PROJECTOR ONE PICTURE, THE VAULT IS FULL, the camera, the audio, the start frame, the reference and the settings - is byte-identical to g2.

**Outcome - both headline defects survived. Do not treat this prompt as the fix.** Frames pulled from the result:
- *The boy still goes front-on and still stops.* From about 3.2s to the last frame he is planted facing the lens, arms at his sides, not moving. The explicit ban did not hold. Two prompt patches have now failed on this one element, which by `feedback_rewrite_when_patching_fails.md` means the next attempt should hoist the boy into his own paragraph and describe his motion by real-world feel rather than adding another ban line - or drop him to one child.
- *The projection is still locked.* Ceiling crops at 3.6 / 4.0 / 4.4 / 4.8s put the sleigh in the same spot on the right slope and the snowflakes in fixed positions; the arcs move with the rafters and nothing travels inside the picture.
- *The door beat still overran* - the hand is on the knob at 1.4s against a 0.4s cap, so the cap did not bind either. The room is only fully in frame from ~2.0s, meaning the picture gets three seconds to move in, the same squeeze as g2.
- What did improve: the girl runs and points throughout, and no glowing strip appeared on the door edge.

The open question this leaves is the one thing I will not decide alone: at 5s the model is spending 1.4s on the door and the remaining 3.6s on two children, and the projection is what it drops. g1 got the projection right at 8s. Either the clip loses an element or it gets its seconds back - Daniel's call.

SUMMARY: two children play in a double-height Christmas great room while a projected Christmas night-sky picture is PLAYING across the back wall and both whole sloping drywall ceiling planes and their dark walnut rafter beams, thrown there by a projector standing in the room. 4 seconds, one continuous handheld phone take, vertical.

GLOBAL STYLE: crisp real iPhone footage at night, handheld, real-time speed. Sharp and in focus from the first frame to the last, at least as sharp as the start image. No blur, no haze, no soft focus, no washed-out exposure, no colour shift.

ASSET ROLES: @Image 1 is frame 0.0s and controls the great room, the cathedral vault of two smooth warm-white drywall slopes with its heavy dark-walnut ridge beam and the dark-walnut rafter beams running up each slope, the white board-and-batten wainscot running round the room, the stacked-ledgestone chimney breast rising the full height at the far end with its chunky dark wood mantel, the white-railed loft balcony high on the right, the two low double-hung windows on the left with snow outside, the lit Christmas tree with its rust-red and antique gold ornaments and its presents, the large light-grey sectional sofa with its big matching ottoman, the jute rug, the light-oak plank floor, the children and the colour.

SUBJECTS:
THE SMALLER CHILD - a boy of about six with curly light brown hair, on the rug in front of the sofa, back to camera and slightly to the side, head tipped back looking up at the projected ceiling with his arms out in wonder, turning slowly on the spot.
THE OTHER CHILD - a girl of about nine with light blonde hair tied in a high ponytail, jumping on the sofa cushions, caught mid-air above them with her knees bent, her arms flung up and her ponytail lifted, the cushion compressed under where she pushed off, bouncing on the spot.
Both in warm rust-red and cream nordic knitted Christmas jumpers with a row of cream snowflakes across the yoke, charcoal knitted lounge trousers and thick oatmeal knitted socks, so their knitted jumpers read clearly. Both stay small in frame, in scale with the huge room. Exactly two children. No third child, no adult, no pet.
THE PROJECTOR - a single Christmas projector switched on and running in the room, out of shot. One lens throws one wide cone of light across the back wall and both whole ceiling slopes. Everything glowing on the drywall, the stone and the timber is that one cone landing.
THE PICTURE - the animated scene that cone is playing: a deep snow-laden pine forest running low along the walls, an ornate fairy-tale Christmas city built of lit gold rising out of the pines on the left with tier upon tier of spires and ribbed onion domes, a long lit steam train winding out of the pines on the right with a bright plume of steam and sparks trailing back over its glowing carriages, wide aurora ribbons in deep crimson and ruby red with warm gold and emerald green threaded between them sweeping up the walls and over both ceiling slopes, one colossal spiral whirlpool of light filling the vault with its centre sitting on the ridge beam, Santa's sleigh drawn by eight flying reindeer with a long tapering trail sweeping out of the mouth of that spiral, a big pale blue full moon glowing high on one side wall with the tiny black silhouette of a second sleigh crossing its face, floating wrapped Christmas gifts drifting at five or six different depths, a dense field of stars of many sizes, long-tailed shooting stars, and a heavy fall of large soft glowing six-point snowflakes and tiny points of light.

HOW THE PICTURE BEHAVES - THIS IS THE ENTIRE POINT OF THE CLIP. In every previous attempt the picture sat dead still like printed wallpaper and the clip was worthless. THE PICTURE IS A FILM PLAYING ON A CINEMA SCREEN: the screen is the drywall, the stone and the timber and holds perfectly still, while everything inside the picture is in constant motion for all four seconds. The start image is one paused frame of that film; at 0.0s it un-pauses. Matching @Image 1 applies to the great room, the furniture, the children and the colours, not to where any element of the picture sits.
THE PICTURE AS A WHOLE IS ANCHORED. It does not rotate, spin, swing, sweep sideways or slide across the room as one sheet. Its outer boundary stays where it is. The motion lives INSIDE it, element by element:
- THE STEAM - the long lit steam train winding out of the pines is working hard and never stops pouring steam. The plume boils out of the funnel, leans back over the carriage roofs, rises about one carriage-height every second, widening and thinning as it climbs and fading out before it reaches the aurora, while a fresh plume is already forming at the funnel behind it. The plume is never a fixed painted shape.
- THE RISING SPARKS - a slow steady rise of glowing gold sparks lifts off the locomotive and climbs the full height of the wall to join the star field, drifting slightly sideways as it goes, new sparks leaving the funnel continuously so the column never empties.
- THE AURORA - each crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbon ripples ALONG ITS OWN LENGTH, a slow travelling wave running from one end of the ribbon to the other, about a third of the ribbon's length every second, the ribbon swelling brighter and thinner as the wave passes and its lower fringe combing and re-forming. Each ribbon's overall arc stays where it is on the drywall; the wave inside it travels. Every ribbon on the wall and on the ceiling is doing this at the same time, and the ribbons that feed into the mouth of the great spiral keep streaming inward along their whole length.
- THE SLEIGH AND THE REINDEER - they are FLYING. The team travels steadily across the ceiling away from the mouth of the great spiral where it sits in the start image, covering about a fifth of the ceiling's width every second, so by 4.0s it has crossed most of the way. The reindeer's legs gallop, their heads nod, the sleigh rocks gently behind them, the long tapering trail streams out behind, and the whole team rises and dips a little as it goes. The team is never parked.
- THE GREAT SPIRAL - the colossal whirlpool of light filling the vault is WINDING and never still. All seven concentric rings turn steadily about the brilliant white-gold core sitting on the ridge beam, each ring creeping inward and tightening as it turns, completing roughly a sixth of a full turn every second, the countless tiny points of light inside them streaming along the curve like a slow river of stars circling a drain. Its outer boundary stays exactly where it is on the ceiling; only the rings inside it wind.
- THE SNOWFLAKES AND THE POINTS OF LIGHT - each one drifts downward and slightly sideways about a quarter of the wall's height every second, the way snow falls past a window at night. Each turns slowly on its own centre as it drifts and breathes brighter and dimmer on its own timing. One that reaches the floor line fades out there while a fresh one fades in high near the ridge beam, so the density holds even.
- THE SMALL TRAVELLERS - the floating wrapped Christmas gifts drift through the scene at their own depths, each one turning slowly end over end as it goes so its ribbon cross and big soft bow swing round into view and away again; the long-tailed shooting stars streak across, their tails thinning out behind them, and fade, with fresh ones setting off elsewhere.
- THE STAR FIELD - the small gold stars hold their places and twinkle, each brightening and dimming on its own timing, never all together.
- THE MOON - the pale blue moon is the ONLY element that holds its position. It stays exactly where it is on the side wall for the whole clip and only brightens and dims very slightly, while the tiny black silhouette of the second sleigh crawls steadily on across its face. Nothing else in the picture is allowed to hold still.
Because the back wall and the whole ceiling are lit by the SAME projector, they are one picture and behave identically at the same instant. Whatever the flakes, the aurora and the stars on the wall are doing, the flakes, the aurora and the stars on the ceiling are doing at the same moment and at the same speed. There is never a still ceiling above a moving wall. Nothing is painted, printed or stencilled on the drywall, the stone or the timber. NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR: the light-oak floorboards, the jute rug, the light-grey sectional sofa, the big ottoman, the tree and the gifts carry no projected shapes at all.

TIMELINE:
0.0-1.0s the person filming takes one clear step forward; the frame moves in and settles. THE OTHER CHILD bounces on the sofa cushions with both arms up, laughing. THE SMALLER CHILD turns on the rug with his face tipped up.
1.0-2.0s the camera holds in the closer position. THE OTHER CHILD pushes up harder and drops back down onto the cushion, her knees bending and springing back. THE SMALLER CHILD keeps turning and tips his head further back.
2.0-4.0s the camera tilts up in one slow smooth move past the top of the tree until the vaulted ceiling fills the upper half of the frame, the aurora rippling along it, the great spiral still winding in it and the sleigh team still flying across it out of the spiral's mouth. Both children stay low in the frame and keep moving.

AUDIO: room tone, two children laughing and squealing, feet thumping on the sofa cushions, feet on a rug. (no BGM). No subtitles.

POSITIVE LOCKS: real-time motion at normal speed throughout - real children moving at real speed with real weight. Exactly two children, and their knitted jumpers read clearly. One continuous take with no cut. The camera makes one move per beat and no more. The picture's outer boundary holds still while every element inside it moves. Walls and ceiling always move together. THE OTHER CHILD stays on the sofa. The picture stays sharp to the last frame.

DO NOT INCLUDE: a frozen or still pattern; a locomotive with no moving steam plume; an aurora that sits still; a sleigh and reindeer parked in one place; a spiral whose rings do not wind; a floating gift that does not drift and turn; flakes that only twinkle without travelling; a still ceiling above moving walls; the whole picture rotating, spinning, sweeping or sliding as one sheet; slow motion; floating or weightless movement; any music; children standing still; a third child; an adult; the projector unit in shot; jellyfish, fish, coral or bubbles; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.
```

## Remake round - 2026-09-20 - the three B-roll 2 frames

Daniel's note, verbatim:

> The rooms don't look right and the animations on the walls doesn't look right at all. They don't look like they emulate on the shape of the walls and ceiling and and they look very washed out, I want them more proeminent not that transparent. I like the type of rooms made for video 7 so you can try more in that style.
>
> Remake them only for video 9 and if I like them you will remake for video 10 too

and, immediately after:

> But not the same house as in video 7, in that style but different house and rooms

Scope: **video 9 only**, three frames - C05 FRAME 2 (the `references/` image, not the starting frame), C06 and C07. Video 10 is untouched and waits on his approval. One generation per shot, then stop.

### Why take 1 failed - the diagnosis

Three causes, compounding. All three are fixed in take 2, and each fix is a specific edit listed below.

1. **The architecture had no relief.** The great room's ceiling was a *flat* coffered grid and the bedroom's was a *smooth* barrel vault. Neither has anything for projected light to break over. A flat plane with shallow mouldings returns a flat picture - which is exactly what "they don't look like they emulate on the shape of the walls and ceiling" describes. Video 7 worked because its rooms carried **heavy dark timber crossing pale boards**: every beam is a hard edge the light has to step across, and a dark timber next to pale plaster is a contrast anchor.
2. **Most of the great room's wall area could not hold a projection.** The back wall was a near-full-height black steel glazed window wall. A dark sheet of glass returns nothing, so the single biggest surface in the shot was dead.
3. **The prompt asked for the wash.** The old `HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE CEILING` block said the light is `TRANSLUCENT`, that the plaster stays `clearly visible THROUGH` it, that every shape is `soft-edged and feathered with no hard opaque border`, and that it gets `steadily fainter, softer and paler ... fading to bare plaster`. Landing that on white-on-white surfaces produces a milky pastel overlay. That is the washed-out look, and it was written into the prompt.

### What changed, per the three-rule reuse protocol

Take 1's prompt text is the source. It was pasted byte-identically and edited with single-match assertions; nothing describing the camera, the children, the pose, the projection *content*, the palette bans or the quality tail was reworded. Six edits per prompt:

| # | paragraph | change |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | opener | the architecture list @Image 2 owns - now the sloping planes, ridge, trusses/rafters, purlins, chimney breast and plain full-height plaster walls |
| 2 | `CHANGE 1 - THE ROOM` | replaced wholesale with the new room, built off the new reference |
| 3 | `TWO THINGS CHANGE FROM @Image 2` | night clause, lamp clause, and the tree / garland / wreath re-anchored onto surfaces that now exist |
| 4 | the surface list in `CHANGE 2` | now names the slopes, the ridge, every truss, every purlin, the chimney breast / the dormer |
| 5 | the aurora, spiral and sleigh clauses | the bands `visibly kinking and changing direction at the ridge` and `visibly stepping and breaking at every truss and every purlin they pass under`; the spiral centred **on the ridge** so half lies on each slope and every ring bends where it crosses |
| 6 | `HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES` | the translucency block deleted and replaced - see below |

The replacement block is the fix for both remaining complaints and it is worth quoting the load-bearing lines:

> THE PROJECTION IS THE BRIGHTEST AND MOST SATURATED THING IN THE FRAME ... it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath almost completely ... It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent ... AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE ... every shape lying on a slope is keystoned and skewed by THAT plane's perspective ... visibly FOLDS at the ridge line, changing direction there with a clean kink, exactly like a picture projected across the inside of a tent ... Every truss, every purlin and every rafter interrupts it ... Falloff is gentle and only in the extreme far corners.

Real interior photographs dropped into `references/` are still the better input and would let the next round go back to them.

Both were generated in v7's *family* - heavy exposed dark timber, a ceiling structure you can read at a glance, big unbroken planes of pale plaster, almost no glazing - but they are a different house and different rooms from v7's.

**`house_great_room_kingpost.png`** - job `18bcaf06-9248-4b95-8400-5e547c79928e`

```text
A plain architectural interior photograph of a real, empty double-height great room in a country house, photographed in flat daylight. This is a reference photograph of ARCHITECTURE ONLY - there is no Christmas decoration, no projection, no coloured light, no people and no clutter of any kind.

THE CEILING, which is the most important thing in the picture: an open gable. Two large flat sloping ceiling planes rise from the tops of the side walls and meet at one high ridge line running the length of the room, so the ceiling is a clear tent shape with two big planes and a hard ridge, not a flat lid and not a curve. Both slopes are lined with wide warm-white painted tongue-and-groove boards running UP the slope from eaves to ridge, so the board lines converge on the ridge and the ridge itself is a single strong straight line across the top of the frame.

THE STRUCTURE: three heavy dark-stained oak king-post trusses cross the room at even spacing, standing proud of the ceiling boards. Each truss is a horizontal tie beam spanning the full width, a vertical king post rising from the middle of the tie beam to the ridge, and two angled struts running from the foot of the king post out to the rafters. Two continuous dark-stained purlins run the whole length of each slope, crossing all three trusses and dividing each slope into three long bands. Every timber is chunky, square-edged, dark chocolate-brown, and casts its own hard shadow onto the pale boards behind it.

THE WALLS: solid warm-white limewashed plaster, full height, deliberately plain and unbroken - big uninterrupted plaster surfaces with no panelling and no large glass. At the far end of the room a grey fieldstone chimney breast rises the full height from floor to ridge, with a thick hand-hewn timber mantel across it and a simple firebox opening below. There is no gallery, no balcony and no staircase in this room - it is one single open volume from floor to ridge, so the side walls are two enormous uninterrupted planes of plaster. There are only two modest windows, both low on the left-hand wall, ordinary rectangular windows with dark frames - no window wall, no glazed gable, no floor-to-ceiling glazing anywhere.

THE FLOOR: wide warm oak floorboards.

Empty room. No furniture except one plain pale sofa and a low wooden coffee table. Flat even daylight coming in from the left, no sunbeams, no lamps lit, no fire lit. Straight-on wide shot from standing height in the middle of the room, looking down the length of the room so both ceiling slopes, the ridge, all three trusses and the chimney breast are clearly visible.

Vertical 9:16 photograph, extremely high quality, tack sharp edge to edge, perfectly exposed, realistic, no distortion, no fisheye, no vignette. No text, no captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.
```

A plain architectural interior photograph of a real, empty bedroom under a steeply pitched roof, photographed in flat daylight. This is a reference photograph of ARCHITECTURE ONLY - there is no Christmas decoration, no projection, no coloured light, no people and no clutter of any kind.

THE CEILING, which is the most important thing in the picture: a steep open pitch. Every timber is chunky, square-edged, dark chocolate-brown, standing proud of the pale boards and casting its own hard shadow onto them.

Flat even daylight from the dormer window, no sunbeams, no lamps lit. Straight-on wide shot from standing height at the foot of the bed, looking down the length of the room so both ceiling slopes, the ridge beam, the rafters, the purlins and the dormer recess are all clearly visible.

Recreate @Image 1's camera, light quality and style in a completely different room, with a much bigger projection. @Image 1 owns the camera, the framing, the poses and the light. @Image 2 is a photograph of a REAL HOUSE and owns the architecture of the room and nothing else - its two sloping ceiling planes, its ridge, its king-post trusses, its purlins, its stone chimney breast, its plain full-height plaster walls and its proportions are to be copied as built; ignore its daylight, its lamps, its time of day and the fact that it is not decorated.

KEEP IDENTICAL: the same vertical 9:16 iPhone photo look, the same camera position and camera height, the same wide framing of a whole tall room seen from a doorway, the same magical low-key evening light in which the only light in the room is the projection itself, and the same two children - a boy standing TURNED AROUND FACING THE CAMERA, front-on, his face fully visible and lit by the coloured light, looking towards the lens with a delighted open-mouthed smile, arms slightly out in wonder, and a girl beside him seen from behind, still looking up at the projected wall.

Wholesome family Christmas scene, children at home with their family at Christmas.

CHANGE 1 - THE ROOM. The room is the real house photographed in @Image 2, seen from the doorway. Build its architecture exactly as it is built there, as a real room a real builder made: a tall double-height great room whose ceiling is one big OPEN GABLE - two large flat sloping ceiling planes rising from the tops of the side walls and meeting at a single high ridge line that runs the whole length of the room away from the camera, both slopes lined with wide warm-white painted tongue-and-groove boards running up the slope from eaves to ridge so the board lines converge on the ridge; three heavy dark chocolate-brown oak king-post trusses cross the room at even spacing and stand proud of those boards, each one a horizontal tie beam spanning the full width, a vertical king post rising from the middle of that tie beam up to the ridge, and two angled struts running from the foot of the king post out to the rafters; two continuous dark purlins run the whole length of each slope, crossing all three trusses and dividing each slope into three long bands; every timber is chunky and square-edged and stands well clear of the pale boards behind it; the side walls are two enormous uninterrupted planes of warm-white limewashed plaster running the full height of the room, with no panelling, no gallery, no balcony and no staircase; at the far end a grey fieldstone chimney breast rises the full height from the floor up into the gable, with a thick hand-hewn timber mantel across it and a simple firebox below; there are only two modest windows in the whole room, both set low on the left-hand wall, ordinary rectangular windows with dark frames, and there is no glazed wall and no glazed gable anywhere. Wide warm oak plank floor, a very large oatmeal linen sectional sofa with a big matching ottoman filling the middle of the room, and a large oat flat-weave rug under it. ABOVE THE FOREST AND THE CITY, wide soft aurora ribbons in emerald green, rose magenta, warm gold and soft teal sweeping up the walls in long slow curves and carrying over onto both sloping ceiling planes, five or six broad overlapping bands layered over each other so there is very little bare board left between them, each band shot through with a fine glittering grain so it reads as a river of stars rather than a painted stripe, where they lie flat ON the slopes and run up them with the direction of the boards instead of hanging as vertical curtains, visibly kinking and changing direction at the ridge where they cross from one slope onto the other, and visibly stepping and breaking at every truss and every purlin they pass under; ACROSS BOTH CEILING SLOPES OVERHEAD, one colossal spiral whirlpool of light filling the whole gable with its centre sitting right on the ridge, so half of it lies on one slope and half on the other and every ring visibly bends where it crosses the ridge - seven concentric glowing rings of emerald green, warm gold and rose magenta winding in on each other in a slow tightening spiral and drawing down to one brilliant white-gold core at the very centre, every ring built of countless tiny points of light so the whole thing reads as a whirlpool of stars rather than a painted disc; floating wrapped Christmas gifts drifting through the whole scene at five or six different depths and sizes, each a solid glowing box in gold, emerald or rose with a bright ribbon cross and a big soft bow on top, the nearest big enough to read the weave of the ribbon, all of them turning slowly end over end as they drift; a dense field of stars at many different sizes, hundreds of them, from tiny pinpricks to a few large bright ones with soft radiating rays, with four or five long-tailed shooting stars crossing at different angles with bright tapering trails; large soft glowing six-point snowflakes in cream and pale gold at several different sizes, the nearest big enough to read every arm, drifting across both ceiling slopes and both side walls; faint wisps of glowing cloud between the aurora bands; and over all of it a heavy fall of hundreds of tiny bright points of light drifting down the walls like real snow caught in a beam. It stays ONE continuous night-sky picture, richly packed and busy, not scattered separate icons. THE PALETTE: emerald green, warm gold, cream white, rose magenta and soft teal, richly saturated against the pale boards, the dark timber and the warm white plaster, with the single pale blue moon as the only cool note anywhere in it. NO grey, no muddy brown, no violet and no harsh electric blue. The great spiral is an AURORA WHIRLPOOL made of Christmas light with a sleigh flying out of its mouth, not a space nebula: it must NOT look like outer space, a galaxy, a wormhole, a tunnel or anything science-fiction, alien or dystopian - it is a storybook Christmas night sky with a lit gold Christmas city, a steam train, flying reindeer and falling gifts in it. Every element is a separate glowing shape sitting on the surface - nothing is painted, printed, stencilled or wallpapered, and nothing is a flat repeating texture. HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS THE BRIGHTEST AND MOST SATURATED THING IN THE FRAME. It is deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength - emerald green, warm gold, cream white and rose magenta at their richest - and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath almost completely, so the plaster and the boards read only as texture and shadow inside the light, not as white surfaces with a faint wash of colour laid over them. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface colour shows through it. Contrast is strong: the bright cores of the gold city, the train, the spiral and the stars are near-white hot, and the gaps between the layers are deep saturated colour, never pale plaster. AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on. Each of the two ceiling slopes is a separate plane at its own angle, and every shape lying on a slope is keystoned and skewed by THAT plane's perspective, so the same aurora band is stretched differently on the left slope than on the right. Every band, every ring of the spiral and every large shape that crosses the ridge visibly FOLDS at the ridge line, changing direction there with a clean kink, exactly like a picture projected across the inside of a tent. Where the light reaches the top of a side wall it breaks again at that corner and runs down the vertical plane at a different angle. Every truss, every purlin and every rafter interrupts it: the face of each timber that points back toward the projector catches its own bright slice of the picture, offset and stepped from the piece on the boards behind it, while the underside and the far side of every timber sit in hard unlit shadow, so the whole ceiling reads as a tent of lit boards crossed by dark timber and never as one flat painted mural. The grey stone chimney breast breaks it again, every stone catching the light on its own face and throwing its own small shadow. The surface texture stays legible inside the light - the board seams, the grain and the square edges of the timbers, the plaster, the stone - so it still reads as projected light on a real room and never as paint, wallpaper, a sticker or a printed panel. Falloff is gentle and only in the extreme far corners; the picture stays strong and fully coloured right across both slopes and both walls. Three or four faint volumetric shafts of coloured light hang visibly in the air of the room, so the beam itself is seen in the space.

CHANGE 4 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The warm oak floorboards, the oat flat-weave rug, the oatmeal sectional sofa, the big ottoman and the tree carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no city, no train, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. Both children catch that spill so their knitted jumpers read clearly.

CHANGE 5 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no round side table, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain rug and floor.

Vertical 9:16 photo, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, natural handheld UGC look, extremely high quality, tack sharp edge to edge, perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, nothing blurry or soft, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

No text, no captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.
```

## Take 3 round - 2026-09-20 - red, opaque, and an American house

Daniel on take 2, verbatim:

> I don't like the rooms because they don't look like real american rooms and
> the animations on the walls and ceilings. The animations on the walls and
> ceiling are too transparent, too washed out and not that impresive. I want
> better animations, more opan but not 100% opac, more interesting, more redish
> and christmas colors, maybe in the style of the attached images but more
> christmas style and redish. Make shure that they emulate on the shape of the
> walls and ceiling.

He attached four screenshots of real projector output: Santa's sleigh and eight
reindeer, colossal spiral whirlpools, aurora ribbons, glowing gold fairy-tale
cities, a lit steam train, a big pale moon with a tiny sleigh crossing it,
floating wrapped gifts, snow-laden pine forests, dense star fields.

**The thing those screenshots actually teach**, and the thing take 2 was
missing: the sky BETWEEN the lit elements is deep near-black. That darkness is
what makes the bright elements burn. Take 2's gaps rendered as glowing white
plaster, which is exactly why it read washed out.

Scope: video 9 only. Video 10 is remade only if Daniel approves these three.

### Why take 2 failed, diagnosed before rewriting anything

|---|---|---|
| "don't look like real american rooms" | the @Image 2 architecture refs were `house_great_room_kingpost.png` (king-post trusses, tongue-and-groove, hand-hewn mantel) and `house_bedroom_purlins.png` (open pitch, exposed purlins) - European lodge vocabulary | two new US-vernacular refs generated: smooth painted drywall instead of planking, walnut-stained rafter beams instead of trusses and purlins, board-and-batten instead of panelled wainscot, stacked ledgestone fireplace, white crown moulding, double-hung windows in white casing, loft balcony with square balusters, light-oak plank floor |
| "too transparent, too washed out" | the opacity paragraph said the projection "overpowers the colour of the surface almost completely" - "almost" left the plaster readable, and nothing in the prompt said what the empty sky should be | new opacity block: ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, backlit stained-glass window, only TEXTURE and SHADOW survive; plus a new palette clause forcing the inter-element sky to near-black blackened indigo and very dark crimson |
| "not that impresive / more redish" | palette was led by emerald, gold, teal | THE PALETTE IS LED BY RED - crimson and ruby dominant, gold second, teal demoted to a thin accent inside one or two aurora bands; spiral rings crimson-to-gold, city red-gold, train windows red-gold, most gifts crimson and gold |
| "make shure that they emulate on the shape of the walls and ceiling" | the wrap paragraph described folding at the ridge but never named a light source, and never asked for cast shadows | new WRAP clause names ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR low in the near corner so everything keystones from one origin, plus a new SHADOW clause: every chunky beam and every raised batten throws a hard-edged shadow band across the surface behind it |

#### `house_great_room_us_vaulted.png` - image prompt

```
A plain real-estate listing photograph of a real, empty family room in an ordinary AMERICAN suburban house in Connecticut, shot for a Zillow listing. It must read unmistakably as a NEW-BUILD AMERICAN HOME, the kind of house you would see in a Zillow listing in a US suburb, and NOT as a European chalet, an alpine lodge, a barn conversion, a farmhouse in the Alps or a rustic cabin.

A tall two-storey family room whose ceiling is one big CATHEDRAL VAULT - two large flat sloping ceiling planes rising from the tops of the side walls and meeting at a single high ridge that runs the whole length of the room away from the camera. Both slopes are SMOOTH WARM-WHITE PAINTED DRYWALL with no planking and no boards. One heavy dark-walnut-stained ridge beam runs the full length of the apex, and four evenly spaced dark-walnut-stained beams run up each slope from the top of the side wall to that ridge beam like rafters, every beam chunky and square-edged and standing well clear of the drywall behind it, with small round recessed can lights set into the slopes between them.

The side walls are smooth warm-white painted drywall above a white-painted BOARD-AND-BATTEN wainscot that runs right round the room to about chest height - evenly spaced vertical white battens standing proud of the wall with a flat white cap rail along the top and a white baseboard below - and wide white crown moulding and white trim throughout.

At the far end a floor-to-ceiling STACKED LEDGESTONE fireplace chimney breast rises the full height into the gable, with a thick chunky dark wood mantel across it and a wide black rectangular firebox below. High on the right-hand side a short second-floor loft balcony overlooks the room behind a white-painted railing of square white balusters.

There are only two modest windows in the whole room, both set low on the left-hand wall, ordinary American double-hung windows with white grilles and wide white casing. There is no glazed wall and no glazed gable anywhere.

Wide light-oak plank floor. A very large light-grey fabric sectional sofa with a big matching ottoman fills the middle of the room, on a large jute rug. The room is undecorated - no Christmas, no people. Daylight.

Vertical 9:16 photo, extremely high quality, tack sharp edge to edge, hyper realistic, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion. No text, no captions, no watermarks.
```

Per CLAUDE.md rule 3, take 3 is take 2 with named substitutions, not a rewrite.
Each prompt was rebuilt programmatically from the take-2 text with an assertion
that every search string matched exactly once, so the untouched paragraphs are
*verified* byte-identical rather than assumed to be.

c06     paragraphs identical: 1, 2, 8, 9   changed: 0, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7

```

The four shared blocks that replaced their take-2 equivalents, quoted so they
can be reused on video 10:

**PALETTE** - replaced *"THE PALETTE: emerald green, warm gold, cream white,
rose magenta and soft teal..."*

> THE PALETTE IS LED BY RED. Deep Christmas crimson and glowing ruby red are the
> dominant colours of the entire picture, warm gold is second, then emerald
> green, cream white and rose magenta; soft teal survives only as a thin accent
> inside one or two of the aurora bands. [...] AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE
> ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit
> element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark
> crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that
> darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.

**OPACITY** - replaced *"THE PROJECTION IS THE BRIGHTEST AND MOST SATURATED
THING IN THE FRAME... OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath almost
completely"*

> THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE [...] It lies on the
> room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window [...] The only things that
> survive through it are the surface's TEXTURE and its SHADOWS [...] That is the
> ten percent; the colour of the surface is not part of it.

This is the literal answer to "more opan but not 100% opac" - it names the
number and it names what the remaining ten percent is allowed to be.

**WRAP** - inserted at the head of the AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE paragraph

> ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of
> this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that
> origin and every surface takes it at its own angle.

**SHADOW** - appended to the same paragraph

> AND EVERY PIECE OF STANDING-PROUD TIMBER AND TRIM THROWS A REAL SHADOW: each
> chunky beam and each raised batten casts a hard-edged shadow of itself across
> the surface behind it, so a dark unlit band runs alongside every beam and every
> batten where the picture is blocked. Those cast shadows are the clearest proof
> that this is light crossing a real room and not a mural, and they must be
> plainly visible.

Two smaller edits, both aimed at "more interesting":

- the aurora bands now *"stream inward and feed into the mouth of the great
  spiral overhead"*, so the ribbons and the whirlpool are one composition
  instead of two unrelated motifs;
- the spiral's seven rings went from *"emerald green, warm gold and rose
  magenta"* to *"deep crimson, ruby red, warm gold and emerald green"*.

And three wording repairs forced by the new architecture: the aurora sentence
no longer says "bare board" or "the direction of the boards" (the new ceiling is
drywall, not planking), and in C06 the stone references were dropped from the
opacity block because that bedroom has no stone in it.

and winding out of the pines on the right a long lit steam train, a dark locomotive leading eight carriages whose windows all glow warm gold, a bright plume of steam and sparks trailing back over the carriage roofs and a glowing gold rail curving away behind it through the trees; ACROSS BOTH CEILING SLOPES OVERHEAD, one colossal spiral whirlpool of light filling the whole vault with its centre sitting right on the ridge beam, so half of it lies on one slope and half on the other and every ring visibly bends where it crosses the ridge - seven concentric glowing rings of deep crimson, ruby red, warm gold and emerald green winding in on each other in a slow tightening spiral and drawing down to one brilliant white-gold core at the very centre, every ring built of countless tiny points of light so the whole thing reads as a whirlpool of stars rather than a painted disc; a big pale blue full moon glowing high on one of the painted drywall side walls, cool silver-blue and softly cratered and the one cool-coloured thing in the whole picture, with the tiny black silhouette of a second sleigh and its reindeer team crossing right over the face of it; floating wrapped Christmas gifts drifting through the whole scene at five or six different depths and sizes, each a solid glowing box in gold, emerald or rose with a bright ribbon cross and a big soft bow on top, the nearest big enough to read the weave of the ribbon, all of them turning slowly end over end as they drift; large soft glowing six-point snowflakes in cream and pale gold at several different sizes, the nearest big enough to read every arm, drifting across both ceiling slopes and both side walls; faint wisps of glowing crimson and gold cloud between the aurora bands; and over all of it a heavy fall of hundreds of tiny bright points of light drifting down the walls like real snow caught in a beam. It stays ONE continuous night-sky picture, richly packed and busy, not scattered separate icons. THE PALETTE IS LED BY RED. Deep Christmas crimson and glowing ruby red are the dominant colours of the entire picture, warm gold is second, then emerald green, cream white and rose magenta; soft teal survives only as a thin accent inside one or two of the aurora bands. The aurora ribbons are mostly crimson and ruby with gold and emerald threaded between them, the outer rings of the great spiral are deep red winding in to a gold core, the fairy-tale city glows red-gold, the train's carriage windows burn red-gold, and most of the floating gifts are crimson and gold. AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle.
