Santa's sleigh drawn by a team of eight flying reindeer sweeping straight out of the mouth of that spiral, seen side-on in glowing warm gold and large enough to read every antler, the harness and bells on each reindeer, the curved runners and Santa himself sitting up in the sleigh with his sack behind him, a long bright tapering trail of golden light streaming back behind the runners into the spiral, the whole team lying flat on one ceiling slope so it is keystoned and stretched by that slope's angle, foreshortening as it recedes along the room and broken into sections by the ridge beam and the rafter beams it passes under; a big pale blue full moon glowing high on one of the painted drywall side walls, cool silver-blue and softly cratered and the one cool-coloured thing in the whole picture, with the tiny black silhouette of a second sleigh and its reindeer team crossing right over the face of it; floating wrapped Christmas gifts drifting through the whole scene at five or six different depths and sizes, each a solid glowing box in gold, emerald or rose with a bright ribbon cross and a big soft bow on top, the nearest big enough to read the weave of the ribbon, all of them turning slowly end over end as they drift; a dense field of stars at many different sizes, hundreds of them, from tiny pinpricks to a few large bright ones with soft radiating rays, with four or five long-tailed shooting stars crossing at different angles with bright tapering trails; large soft glowing six-point snowflakes in cream and pale gold at several different sizes, the nearest big enough to read every arm, drifting across both ceiling slopes and both side walls; faint wisps of glowing crimson and gold cloud between the aurora bands; and over all of it a heavy fall of hundreds of tiny bright points of light drifting down the walls like real snow caught in a beam. It stays ONE continuous night-sky picture, richly packed and busy, not scattered separate icons. THE PALETTE IS LED BY RED. Deep Christmas crimson and glowing ruby red are the dominant colours of the entire picture, warm gold is second, then emerald green, cream white and rose magenta; soft teal survives only as a thin accent inside one or two of the aurora bands. The aurora ribbons are mostly crimson and ruby with gold and emerald threaded between them, the outer rings of the great spiral are deep red winding in to a gold core, the fairy-tale city glows red-gold, the train's carriage windows burn red-gold, and most of the floating gifts are crimson and gold. Everything is richly saturated, and the single pale blue moon is the only cool note anywhere in it. NO grey, no muddy brown, no violet and no harsh electric blue. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn. The great spiral is an AURORA WHIRLPOOL made of Christmas light with a sleigh flying out of its mouth, not a space nebula: it must NOT look like outer space, a galaxy, a wormhole, a tunnel or anything science-fiction, alien or dystopian - it is a storybook Christmas night sky with a lit gold Christmas city, a steam train, flying reindeer and falling gifts in it. Every element is a separate glowing shape sitting on the surface - nothing is painted, printed, stencilled or wallpapered, and nothing is a flat repeating texture. HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely. The white painted drywall, the white battens and the pale stone do NOT read as white anywhere inside the projection field - the light has swallowed their colour. The only things that survive through it are the surface's TEXTURE and its SHADOWS: the slight tooth of the drywall, the joints and edges, the mortar lines of the stone, the square corners of the beams and battens. That is the ten percent; the colour of the surface is not part of it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white. Contrast is extreme: the cores of the gold city, the train, the spiral and the stars are near-white hot, the mid-tones are deep saturated crimson and emerald, and the empty sky between them is almost black - never pale paint. AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on. Each of the two ceiling slopes is a separate plane at its own angle, and every shape lying on a slope is keystoned and skewed by THAT plane's perspective, so the same aurora band is stretched differently on the left slope than on the right. Every band, every ring of the spiral and every large shape that crosses the ridge visibly FOLDS at the ridge line, changing direction there with a clean kink, exactly like a picture projected across the inside of a tent. Where the light reaches the top of a side wall it breaks again at that corner and runs down the vertical plane at a different angle. The ridge beam and every rafter beam interrupt it: the face of each beam that points back toward the projector catches its own bright slice of the picture, offset and stepped from the piece on the drywall behind it, while the underside and the far side of every beam sit in hard unlit shadow, so the whole ceiling reads as a lit vault ribbed by dark beams and never as one flat painted mural. The white board-and-batten wainscot breaks it again, every raised batten catching its own bright vertical strip and throwing its own shadow so the picture ripples across the lower wall in a row of ribs. The stacked-stone chimney breast breaks it a third time, every stone catching the light on its own face and throwing its own small shadow, and the white loft railing breaks it a fourth, each square baluster catching a bright edge and casting a bar of shadow onto the wall behind it. The surface texture stays legible inside the light - the tooth of the drywall, the square edges of the beams and battens, the mortar joints of the stone - so it still reads as projected light on a real room and never as paint, wallpaper, a sticker or a printed panel. AND EVERY PIECE OF STANDING-PROUD TIMBER AND TRIM THROWS A REAL SHADOW: each chunky beam and each raised batten casts a hard-edged shadow of itself across the surface behind it, so a dark unlit band runs alongside every beam and every batten where the picture is blocked. Those cast shadows are the clearest proof that this is light crossing a real room and not a mural, and they must be plainly visible. Falloff is gentle and only in the extreme far corners; the picture stays strong and fully coloured right across both slopes and both walls. Three or four faint volumetric shafts of coloured light hang visibly in the air of the room, so the beam itself is seen in the space.

CHANGE 4 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The light-oak floorboards, the jute rug, the light-grey sectional sofa, the big ottoman and the tree carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no city, no train, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. Both children catch that spill so their knitted jumpers read clearly.

CHANGE 5 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no round side table, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain jute rug and floor.

Vertical 9:16 photo, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, natural handheld UGC look, extremely high quality, tack sharp edge to edge, perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, nothing blurry or soft, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

No text, no captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.
```

## Take 4 round - 2026-09-20 - full coverage and razor sharpness

**Daniel's note, verbatim:** "I like the better this time, but I want them
sharper and they should cover the entire walls and ceiling. Look at C5 - in the
front, above the fireplace there is no animation on the wall and the same for
C8. I don't want them so blurish, they should be sharper but I see
improvements."

**"C8" read as C7.** Video 9 has C01-C07 only. C06 is the bedroom and has no
fireplace; C07 is the great room sharing C05's stacked-stone chimney breast.
So the second fireplace shot he means is C07.

**What was wrong in take 3.** Both C05 and C07 came back with the tall
stacked-stone chimney breast running up the centre of the frame carrying almost
no projection, and the lower halves of the side walls and the wall under the
loft were thin too. Root cause: take 3's content layout is forest-low /
city-left / train-right / aurora-mid / spiral-overhead and nothing in it ever
lands on the centre pillar; the prompt named the chimney breast as part of the
field but never said it had to be *filled*. The line "Falloff is gentle and only
in the extreme far corners" additionally licensed dim edges. The softness he
calls blur is bloom and glow haze, not focus.

**Derivation.** Take 4 is take 3 with exactly five substitutions per shot, each
asserted to match exactly once, so every paragraph not named below is *verified*
byte-identical to take 3 rather than assumed to be. Paragraph diff:

```
c05f2   identical: [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 9]  changed: [5, 8]  chars 16786 -> 19315
c06     identical: [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 9]  changed: [5, 8]  chars 16676 -> 18848
c07     identical: [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 9]  changed: [5, 8]  chars 17285 -> 19814
```

Only the projection block (para 5) and the final quality line (para 8) moved.
The American architecture, the red-led palette, the ninety-percent-opaque
block, the near-black sky between elements, the single-projector keystone
origin and the cast-shadow clause all survive untouched.

The five edits:

1. **Coverage.** After "with no visible edge, no rectangle and no screen."
   inserted a NO SURFACE IN THE ROOM IS LEFT BARE block. The great-room version
   names THE TALL STACKED-STONE CHIMNEY BREAST ABOVE THE FIREPLACE explicitly -
   the picture crosses it from the mantel shelf to the ceiling ridge as densely
   as everywhere else and is never a bare grey column - plus the wainscot, the
   wall behind the loft railing, the loft soffit, the strips beside the windows
   and the right-hand wall. The bedroom version names the gable wall behind the
   bed, every raised batten on it, both knee walls, the strips beside the
   window, the right-hand side wall and both ceiling slopes into the corners.
2. **Falloff.** "Falloff is gentle and only in the extreme far corners..." ->
   "There is NO falloff anywhere... no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and
   no bare patch at any boundary of the room."
3. **Sharpness.** The volumetric-shafts sentence now constrains the shafts to
   "narrow and crisply defined" so they cannot fog the picture, and carries an
   EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP block: hard crisp edges on
   every named element, points of light as DISCRETE PINPOINTS not blobs, and
   explicit bans on bloom, glow halo, lens flare, atmospheric haze, soft-focus
   glaze, gaussian softness, depth-of-field blur and bokeh.
4. **Quality line.** "tack sharp edge to edge" -> "RAZOR SHARP EDGE TO EDGE AND
   CORNER TO CORNER at full 2K resolution with fine micro-detail legible
   everywhere".
5. **Blur ban.** "zero motion blur, nothing blurry or soft" -> "zero motion
   blur, zero depth-of-field blur, zero bokeh, zero bloom, zero glow haze, zero
   soft focus, nothing blurry, nothing smeared and nothing soft or gauzy
   anywhere in the frame".

HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely. The white painted drywall, the white battens and the pale stone do NOT read as white anywhere inside the projection field - the light has swallowed their colour. The only things that survive through it are the surface's TEXTURE and its SHADOWS: the slight tooth of the drywall, the joints and edges, the mortar lines of the stone, the square corners of the beams and battens. That is the ten percent; the colour of the surface is not part of it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white. Contrast is extreme: the cores of the gold city, the train, the spiral and the stars are near-white hot, the mid-tones are deep saturated crimson and emerald, and the empty sky between them is almost black - never pale paint. AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on. Each of the two ceiling slopes is a separate plane at its own angle, and every shape lying on a slope is keystoned and skewed by THAT plane's perspective, so the same aurora band is stretched differently on the left slope than on the right. Every band, every ring of the spiral and every large shape that crosses the ridge visibly FOLDS at the ridge line, changing direction there with a clean kink, exactly like a picture projected across the inside of a tent. Where the light reaches the top of a side wall it breaks again at that corner and runs down the vertical plane at a different angle. The ridge beam and every rafter beam interrupt it: the face of each beam that points back toward the projector catches its own bright slice of the picture, offset and stepped from the piece on the drywall behind it, while the underside and the far side of every beam sit in hard unlit shadow, so the whole ceiling reads as a lit vault ribbed by dark beams and never as one flat painted mural. The white board-and-batten wainscot breaks it again, every raised batten catching its own bright vertical strip and throwing its own shadow so the picture ripples across the lower wall in a row of ribs. The stacked-stone chimney breast breaks it a third time, every stone catching the light on its own face and throwing its own small shadow, and the white loft railing breaks it a fourth, each square baluster catching a bright edge and casting a bar of shadow onto the wall behind it. The surface texture stays legible inside the light - the tooth of the drywall, the square edges of the beams and battens, the mortar joints of the stone - so it still reads as projected light on a real room and never as paint, wallpaper, a sticker or a printed panel. AND EVERY PIECE OF STANDING-PROUD TIMBER AND TRIM THROWS A REAL SHADOW: each chunky beam and each raised batten casts a hard-edged shadow of itself across the surface behind it, so a dark unlit band runs alongside every beam and every batten where the picture is blocked. Those cast shadows are the clearest proof that this is light crossing a real room and not a mural, and they must be plainly visible. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the top corners of the vault, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room. Three or four thin volumetric shafts of coloured light hang visibly in the air of the room so the beam itself is seen in the space, but they are narrow and crisply defined and they do NOT fog, veil or soften any part of the picture. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a deep snow-laden pine forest running the whole width of the room along the very bottom of the projection, rank behind rank of little glowing firs in emerald green and warm gold with snow heaped on every branch, and rising straight out of that forest on the left a whole ornate fairy-tale Christmas city built of lit gold - tier upon tier of slender spires, ribbed onion domes, arched galleries and pinnacled towers stacked up the wall, every arched window lit warm gold, fine gold filigree running along every cornice and balustrade, thick snow lying on every ledge and roof and dome, the whole city glowing as though it were carved out of light; and winding out of the pines on the right a long lit steam train, a dark locomotive leading eight carriages whose windows all glow warm gold, a bright plume of steam and sparks trailing back over the carriage roofs and a glowing gold rail curving away behind it through the trees; ABOVE THE FOREST AND THE CITY, wide soft aurora ribbons in deep Christmas crimson and glowing ruby red, with warm gold, emerald green and rose magenta threaded between them and only a thin thread of teal, sweeping up the walls in long slow curves and carrying over onto both sloping ceiling planes, five or six broad overlapping bands layered over each other so there is very little bare surface left between them, each band shot through with a fine glittering grain so it reads as a river of stars rather than a painted stripe, where they lie flat ON the slopes and run up them with the direction of the slope instead of hanging as vertical curtains, visibly kinking and changing direction at the ridge beam where they cross from one slope onto the other, visibly stepping and breaking at every rafter beam they pass under, and visibly rippling across the raised white battens of the wainscot where they reach the lower wall, and several of these red and gold ribbons stream inward and feed into the mouth of the great spiral overhead; ACROSS BOTH CEILING SLOPES OVERHEAD, one colossal spiral whirlpool of light filling the whole vault with its centre sitting right on the ridge beam, so half of it lies on one slope and half on the other and every ring visibly bends where it crosses the ridge - seven concentric glowing rings of deep crimson, ruby red, warm gold and emerald green winding in on each other in a slow tightening spiral and drawing down to one brilliant white-gold core at the very centre, every ring built of countless tiny points of light so the whole thing reads as a whirlpool of stars rather than a painted disc; Santa's sleigh drawn by a team of eight flying reindeer sweeping straight out of the mouth of that spiral, seen side-on in glowing warm gold and large enough to read every antler, the harness and bells on each reindeer, the curved runners and Santa himself sitting up in the sleigh with his sack behind him, a long bright tapering trail of golden light streaming back behind the runners into the spiral, the whole team lying flat on one ceiling slope so it is keystoned and stretched by that slope's angle, foreshortening as it recedes along the room and broken into sections by the ridge beam and the rafter beams it passes under; a big pale blue full moon glowing high on one of the painted drywall side walls, cool silver-blue and softly cratered and the one cool-coloured thing in the whole picture, with the tiny black silhouette of a second sleigh and its reindeer team crossing right over the face of it; floating wrapped Christmas gifts drifting through the whole scene at five or six different depths and sizes, each a solid glowing box in gold, emerald or rose with a bright ribbon cross and a big soft bow on top, the nearest big enough to read the weave of the ribbon, all of them turning slowly end over end as they drift; a dense field of stars at many different sizes, hundreds of them, from tiny pinpricks to a few large bright ones with soft radiating rays, with four or five long-tailed shooting stars crossing at different angles with bright tapering trails; large soft glowing six-point snowflakes in cream and pale gold at several different sizes, the nearest big enough to read every arm, drifting across both ceiling slopes and both side walls; faint wisps of glowing crimson and gold cloud between the aurora bands; and over all of it a heavy fall of hundreds of tiny bright points of light drifting down the walls like real snow caught in a beam. It stays ONE continuous night-sky picture, richly packed and busy, not scattered separate icons. THE PALETTE IS LED BY RED. Deep Christmas crimson and glowing ruby red are the dominant colours of the entire picture, warm gold is second, then emerald green, cream white and rose magenta; soft teal survives only as a thin accent inside one or two of the aurora bands. The aurora ribbons are mostly crimson and ruby with gold and emerald threaded between them, the outer rings of the great spiral are deep red winding in to a gold core, the fairy-tale city glows red-gold, the train's carriage windows burn red-gold, and most of the floating gifts are crimson and gold. Everything is richly saturated, and the single pale blue moon is the only cool note anywhere in it. NO grey, no muddy brown, no violet and no harsh electric blue. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn. The great spiral is an AURORA WHIRLPOOL made of Christmas light with a sleigh flying out of its mouth, not a space nebula: it must NOT look like outer space, a galaxy, a wormhole, a tunnel or anything science-fiction, alien or dystopian - it is a storybook Christmas night sky with a lit gold Christmas city, a steam train, flying reindeer and falling gifts in it. Every element is a separate glowing shape sitting on the surface - nothing is painted, printed, stencilled or wallpapered, and nothing is a flat repeating texture. HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely. The white painted drywall, the white battens and the pale stone do NOT read as white anywhere inside the projection field - the light has swallowed their colour. The only things that survive through it are the surface's TEXTURE and its SHADOWS: the slight tooth of the drywall, the joints and edges, the mortar lines of the stone, the square corners of the beams and battens. That is the ten percent; the colour of the surface is not part of it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white. Contrast is extreme: the cores of the gold city, the train, the spiral and the stars are near-white hot, the mid-tones are deep saturated crimson and emerald, and the empty sky between them is almost black - never pale paint. AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on. Each of the two ceiling slopes is a separate plane at its own angle, and every shape lying on a slope is keystoned and skewed by THAT plane's perspective, so the same aurora band is stretched differently on the left slope than on the right. Every band, every ring of the spiral and every large shape that crosses the ridge visibly FOLDS at the ridge line, changing direction there with a clean kink, exactly like a picture projected across the inside of a tent. Where the light reaches the top of a side wall it breaks again at that corner and runs down the vertical plane at a different angle. The ridge beam and every rafter beam interrupt it: the face of each beam that points back toward the projector catches its own bright slice of the picture, offset and stepped from the piece on the drywall behind it, while the underside and the far side of every beam sit in hard unlit shadow, so the whole ceiling reads as a lit vault ribbed by dark beams and never as one flat painted mural. The white board-and-batten wainscot breaks it again, every raised batten catching its own bright vertical strip and throwing its own shadow so the picture ripples across the lower wall in a row of ribs. The stacked-stone chimney breast breaks it a third time, every stone catching the light on its own face and throwing its own small shadow, and the white loft railing breaks it a fourth, each square baluster catching a bright edge and casting a bar of shadow onto the wall behind it. The surface texture stays legible inside the light - the tooth of the drywall, the square edges of the beams and battens, the mortar joints of the stone - so it still reads as projected light on a real room and never as paint, wallpaper, a sticker or a printed panel. AND EVERY PIECE OF STANDING-PROUD TIMBER AND TRIM THROWS A REAL SHADOW: each chunky beam and each raised batten casts a hard-edged shadow of itself across the surface behind it, so a dark unlit band runs alongside every beam and every batten where the picture is blocked. Those cast shadows are the clearest proof that this is light crossing a real room and not a mural, and they must be plainly visible. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the top corners of the vault, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room. Three or four thin volumetric shafts of coloured light hang visibly in the air of the room so the beam itself is seen in the space, but they are narrow and crisply defined and they do NOT fog, veil or soften any part of the picture. EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP. Each spire and arched window of the gold city, each carriage window of the train, each antler and harness bell on each reindeer, each arm of each snowflake, each ribbon and bow on each floating gift and each ring of the great spiral has a hard, crisp, precisely drawn edge. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 4 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The light-oak floorboards, the jute rug, the light-grey sectional sofa, the big ottoman and the tree carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no city, no train, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. Both children catch that spill so their knitted jumpers read clearly.

CHANGE 5 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no round side table, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain jute rug and floor.

Vertical 9:16 photo, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, natural handheld UGC look, extremely high quality, RAZOR SHARP EDGE TO EDGE AND CORNER TO CORNER at full 2K resolution with fine micro-detail legible everywhere - the knit stitches of the jumpers, the grain of the floorboards, the tooth of the drywall, the joints of the stone and every individual pinpoint of the projection all crisply resolved - perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, zero depth-of-field blur, zero bokeh, zero bloom, zero glow haze, zero soft focus, nothing blurry, nothing smeared and nothing soft or gauzy anywhere in the frame, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

No text, no captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.
```

## C01 remake - 2026-09-20 - the hook concept was changed

The store end-cap hook (g1-g6) was abandoned. Daniel: *"We couldn't get the hook
right so we will change it."* The new hook is a **1986-style tangled-lights
frustration cold open**, built off a reel he supplied.

### The reference - `references/v9_c01_r3.png`

Daniel's screenshot of the reel, captioned *"Christmas Decor in 1986"*: a woman
kneeling on a hardwood floor about to push a huge tangled knot of vintage
multicoloured string lights into a low wall socket, two small children reacting
behind her, a decorated tree on the right, shot from a hallway past a white wall
corner.

468 x 833 RGBA, 609393 bytes, md5 `52e6246889bf50007d1213a9e8bc05ef`.
Higgsfield media id `4e905564-7c69-42dd-9ba5-c6333626379d`, attached to both
generations below in the `image` role.

### The brief

> "I want something exactly like the attached image, different women different
> kids and different room. Also the attached reference image has one more
> problem, the light the women want to plug into electricity are not connected
> to the christmas tree. Generate me 2 options. The images must be extremely
> high quality, perfect light, iphone shots, nothing blurish. Use gpt image 2.5
> Sunburst, 2k, mid quality settings, 9x16."

So: **copy the camera and the staging, replace the people and the room, and fix
the continuity defect** - the string she is plugging in has to visibly be the
tree's string.

### The one judgement call in this round

If that string really is the tree's string, then **the tree cannot already be
lit** in the starting frame - the plug is still in her hand. Both options
therefore render a fully decorated but **unlit** tree, with one unbroken run of
flex leading from the plug, through the knot, across the floor, up into the
lower branches. This also matches the standing rule that a starting frame is the
*before* state and the reveal happens in the video.

The alternative reading - tree already glowing, the knot being a second string -
is a one-line change to the `THE TREE IS NOT SWITCHED ON` block and was flagged
to Daniel rather than decided here.

### What the two options share, byte for byte

Everything describing camera, geometry, the knot, the connection, the unlit
tree, colour, lighting/quality, the caption band and the negative tail is
**identical text in both prompts**. Only four blocks differ: `THE ROOM`,
`THE TREE`, `THE MOTHER`, `THE CHILDREN`. Variation was put into the set and the
cast, never into the camera language - rewording geometry to manufacture
variation is the documented way this drive loses a framing it already has.

### Settings - both generations

`gpt_image_2_5` - variant `sunburst` - quality `medium` - resolution `2k` -
aspect_ratio `9:16` - count 1 - `use_unlim` false - workspace Oneofone1
(`b2c97bd7-fedf-489d-b211-3de4e39dc90a`). Output 1520 x 2688.

### Option A - `starting_frames/v9_c01_g7.png`

Warm greige living room, chestnut-haired mother in a cream Fair Isle, curly-
haired girl in red tartan, boy in reindeer pyjamas.

Job `449ed0de-a806-4966-b15f-53bac6662a33`. 6449842 bytes, md5
`7236729e16cbc6d5678c495db9ceb80e`.

#### Image prompt

```
Extremely high quality candid vertical iPhone photo taken from a hallway looking into a family's living room on a winter evening: a young mother kneels on the bare floor with a huge tangled knot of old multicoloured Christmas string lights in front of her, her arm stretched out to push the plug into a wall socket, while her two small children stand behind her watching the Christmas tree.

Use the attached reference photograph for BOTH the staging AND the camera position - the mother kneeling in the near foreground, the knot of lights on the floor beside her, her arm reaching out to the low wall socket, the two small children standing behind her, the tall Christmas tree on the right and the plain white wall corner the camera is looking past. COPY ITS CAMERA POSITION AND ITS COMPOSITION EXACTLY. Copy nothing else from it: the room, the mother and the children are all described below and are all different people in a different house.

FRAMING AND ANGLE - COPY THE CAMERA FROM THE REFERENCE: the camera is handheld at about the chest height of a standing adult, tilted very slightly down, standing in the next room and looking in through a doorway, so a plain white wall corner with a white skirting board runs floor to ceiling down the RIGHT EDGE of the frame and takes up roughly the right eighth of the picture - we are looking past it into the living room. The living room floor fills the bottom half of the frame and runs away into depth. The mother kneels in the lower LEFT of the frame, seen from behind at three quarters, close to the camera and large in the picture: her head and shoulders sit around the middle of the frame and the bottom edge of the picture cuts her just below the knees. The knot of lights lies on the bare floor immediately to her right, between her and that white wall corner, around the centre of the lower third of the picture. The Christmas tree stands on the RIGHT of the frame just inside the white corner, tall enough that the top edge of the picture cuts off its top. The two children stand on the floor in the middle distance between the mother and the tree, small in the frame, their heads in the middle third of the picture. Nobody is looking at the camera and nobody is posing. This is NOT a side angle, NOT a low floor-level angle and NOT a symmetrical composition.

THE KNOT AND THE PLUG: on the bare floorboards immediately to the mother's right lies a big ugly knot of old-fashioned Christmas string lights - dark green flex wound and knotted into a messy nest about the width of two floorboards across, studded with fat vintage multicoloured bulbs in red, green, blue and yellow, with a white in-line plug body and loops of slack wire lifting out of the top of it. She holds the end of that flex in her outstretched right hand and the two-pin plug is an inch from a white electrical socket set low on the white wall corner at the right of the frame. It is not plugged in yet and every bulb in the knot is dark.

THE CONNECTION - THIS IS THE IMPORTANT PART: one single continuous run of that SAME dark green flex with the SAME fat multicoloured bulbs leaves the far side of the knot, travels away across the floorboards to the right, passes behind the children, reaches the foot of the Christmas tree and climbs up into its lower branches, where it is plainly the very same string that is wound round and round the whole tree above. Same wire colour, same bulb size, same bulb spacing, one unbroken line. The eye must be able to follow the wire from the plug in her hand, through the knot on the floor, across the floor and up onto the tree without a single break or gap. The heap on the floor is NOT a separate loose pile with nothing leading out of it, and the tree is NOT wired to anything else.

THE TREE IS NOT SWITCHED ON: because the plug is still in her hand, every bulb on the tree is dark and unlit. The tree is not glowing, no light comes off it, and it throws no coloured light onto the floor, the walls or the children. It is still fully decorated and still reads as a beautiful Christmas tree - the baubles catch the warm lamplight in the room - it is simply not lit yet.

THE ROOM: a cosy American family living room. Walls painted soft warm greige above a white-painted panelled dado and white skirting, wide mid-brown oak floorboards with a soft satin sheen running away from the camera, a caramel-tan leather two-seat sofa along the left wall with a cream chunky-knit throw over its arm, a faded red-and-blue traditional patterned rug on the floor in front of it, a walnut side table behind the sofa carrying a brass table lamp with a cream drum shade that is switched on and glowing, and three cream knitted stockings hanging from a white-painted mantel on the back wall.

THE TREE: a big real spruce, dense and dark green, dressed with red and gold glass baubles, small cream wooden stars, a red tartan ribbon wound down it and a gold star on top, with a thick red velvet tree skirt pooled on the floor at its base and three presents wrapped in kraft paper and red twine sitting on it.

THE MOTHER: about 32, dark chestnut brown hair falling loose to the middle of her back with a few strands across her face, an oversized cream Fair Isle sweater with bands of red and forest green snowflakes across the chest and sleeves, dark indigo jeans and thick cream ribbed socks, no shoes. She kneels sitting back on her heels, her left hand flat on the floorboards taking her weight, her right arm stretched right out towards the socket with the plug pinched between her thumb and fingers, her head turned in profile watching the plug. She is concentrating, slightly fed up, not smiling.

THE CHILDREN: a girl of about six standing on the rug behind her mother, dark curly hair in two high puffs tied with red ribbon, a red tartan flannel pyjama set, barefoot, her mouth wide open in a huge exaggerated gasp of amazement and both hands up near her cheeks, staring at the tree. Beside her a boy of about four with straight black hair cut in a fringe, green pyjamas printed with small reindeer, crouched down on one knee with his right arm flung out pointing straight at the tree. Neither child blocks the mother, the knot of lights or the tree.

COLOUR: warm domestic evening white balance, the way an iPhone renders a lamp-lit living room - warm but not orange. The picture is NOT sepia, NOT amber-washed and NOT heavily graded. Skin reads natural, the white wall corner reads clean white, the floorboards read as real wood and the sweater reads as true cream.

LIGHTING AND QUALITY: the room is lit by the warm table lamp plus soft ambient light - cosy and warm, but still bright enough that every face, every twist of wire in the knot and the whole tree are clearly readable, with no dark corners where detail disappears and no blown highlights. Shot on an iPhone 16 Pro and straight out of the camera roll - an ordinary everyday snapshot somebody took of their family, not a magazine photo and not a rendered image. Extremely high quality, tack sharp and in focus everywhere from the mother in the foreground to the tree behind her, crisp detail in every individual bulb and every twist of flex in the tangle, realistic skin, hair and knitwear texture. Absolutely nothing blurry: no motion blur, no soft focus, no background blur, no bokeh, no grain, no film look, no cinematic grade, no vignette. Normal wide-angle lens, vertical lines vertical and parallel, no fisheye distortion.

FRAMING: leave the upper LEFT of the image - roughly the top eighth of the frame on the wall side - as plain uncluttered wall, free of pictures, shelves, garland, hanging decorations and lettering, so a caption can sit there. Do not pull the camera back or shrink the mother to make that room.

No captions, no watermarks, no added lettering anywhere in the image, no writing on any clothing, no user-interface elements, no mute or speaker icons and no social-media overlays.
```

### Take numbering

g1-g5 are absent from `starting_frames/` on purpose - they were rejected earlier
today and staged in `to_delete/thexmasglow_c01_rejected_2026-09-20/`, recorded in
`content/lost_takes.txt`. g6 is the surviving store end-cap frame. The remake
therefore continues at **g7 and g8**; numbers are never reused.

### What came back

Both frames rendered at 1520 x 2688, sharp, unlit tree, wire continuous from the
plug through the knot and off to the right toward the tree base in both.

Nothing has been written to `video_board.html` - the board is only touched once
Daniel has picked one.

### One transfer defect worth recording

The first copy of g8 onto the drive landed **truncated** - 5799936 bytes on disk
against 6268195 at source, and PIL refused to decode it (`image file is
truncated`). It was re-copied from the CDN and re-verified by md5. This is the
same mount truncation described in `CLAUDE.md`; it applies to binaries copied
with `cp`, not only to text written through the file tools. **md5 both ends after
every copy, images included.**

### Video prompt - clips/v9_c01_g5.mp4

Daniel picked **g8** on 2026-09-20 and gave the animation brief verbatim:

> Held iphone shot, no zooms, no cuts. The women plugs the lights into the power
> outlet, and exactly when she blugs it the christmas tree starts burning on
> fire. The women and the kids are imidiatly shoked and run towords the camera,
> the person filming also moves backwords imidiatly after the christmas tree
> starts burning. The person filming it's a male and exactly as the christmas
> tree starts to burn on fire screams "Oh, shoot"
> Hyperrealistic.
> Use seedance 2.5, 1080p, 5s

**Start frame:** `starting_frames/v9_c01_g8.png` - Higgsfield media
`76e29118-dbc9-4087-b4c7-7c786041615f`, passed as a ROLE inside `medias`
(`{"value": "...", "role": "start_image"}`), never as a top-level `start_image`.

**Settings** - `model seedance_2_5`, `mode omni_reference`, `aspect_ratio 9:16`,
`resolution 1080p`, `duration 5`, `generate_audio true`, `count 1`,
`use_unlim false`, `declined_preset_id 24bae836-2c4a-48e0-89b6-49fcc0b21612`
sent pre-emptively. Accepted first try, no preset refusal.
Job `4b57f2c4-12a6-404e-8d4b-555563d43f85`.

Two house rules shaped the wording. "Held iPhone" is never written into a POV
prompt - it renders an actual phone into the bottom of the frame - so the camera
is declared to BE the phone and a hard negative follows. And everything Daniel
wanted to happen in the same instant (pins seating, bulbs flashing, tree
igniting, the man shouting, the camera jolting) was fused into ONE sentence
about ONE frame, because the C01 take-3 to take-4 lesson on this shot is that
simultaneous events written as separate sentences render as separate events.

```
Single continuous handheld iPhone shot, 5 seconds, vertical 9:16, hyperrealistic. No cuts, no zoom, no slow motion, no speed ramp. The camera itself IS the phone being held by a man standing in the hallway doorway, so we see ONLY what he sees. No phone, no screen, no hand, no fingers, no thumb, no arm, no sleeve, no device and no object ever appears in the foreground or along any edge of the frame.

Start frame: a blonde mother in a forest-green cable-knit sweater kneels on pale wooden floorboards in the lower left, her right arm stretched out to a white wall socket on the white corner at the right of the frame, holding the plug of a big tangled knot of unlit multicoloured Christmas string lights that lies on the floor beside her. One continuous run of that same string leads away across the floor and up into the decorated but unlit Christmas tree standing on the right. A boy in red-and-white striped pyjamas and a small girl in a cream nightgown stand behind her near the navy sofa.

ACTION TIMELINE:
0.0-0.8s - The mother pushes the plug the last few centimetres into the socket. Her hand moves slowly and steadily, her head still turned in profile watching it. Everything else in the room is calm and still. The tree is dark. The camera is locked where it stands in the start frame, only natural handheld sway.

AT 0.8s - ONE SINGLE INSTANT, AND EVERYTHING IN IT HAPPENS ON THE SAME FRAME. The two pins seat in the socket; every bulb on the knot and on the tree flashes on for a split second; and in that same instant a burst of orange flame erupts low in the Christmas tree where the string meets the branches and the tree catches fire. On that exact same frame the man holding the camera shouts "Oh, shoot" and the whole frame jolts as he flinches backwards. The fire is not delayed, it does not build up, it does not start small and grow for a second first - the tree is already burning on the same frame the plug goes in.

0.8-2.0s - The flames climb the tree fast and realistically, running up through the dry branches, orange and yellow, throwing real flickering firelight across the shiplap walls, the floorboards, the sofa and the three faces, with dark smoke rolling up off the top of the tree toward the ceiling. Ornaments glow and the branches blacken. The mother recoils hard away from the socket, twists round onto her hands and pushes herself up off the floor. The boy and the girl scream and turn toward the camera. From this moment the camera is retreating backwards down the hallway away from the doorway in fast unsteady handheld steps, the whole frame shaking and swinging as the man walks backwards.

2.0-5.0s - The mother is on her feet and runs straight toward the camera with her arms out, scooping the two children ahead of her, all three sprinting toward the lens and growing rapidly larger in the frame, faces wide with panic, the boy's arms pumping, the little girl half carried. The camera keeps backing away from them the whole time so they never quite reach it, the doorway and the burning tree receding behind them, firelight flaring across the hallway walls. End frame: the three of them close and large in the frame mid-run, the burning tree small and bright behind them through the doorway.

AUDIO: natural room sound only. One male voice, off camera behind the lens, shouting "Oh, shoot" exactly on the frame the tree catches fire - nothing else from him before it, nothing else after it. Then the crackle and roar of the fire, the mother shouting wordlessly, the two children screaming, bare feet running on wood. NO music, no score, no background song, no narration, no added sound effects beyond these.

REALISM - HARD RULES: the mother, the boy and the girl keep exactly the same faces, hair, clothes and body shapes as the start frame throughout. The room stays the same room - same cream shiplap walls, same navy sofa with the mustard blanket, same pale floorboards, same rug, same lamp, same window. The fire behaves like real fire on a real dry conifer: it spreads along branches, it does not float, it does not appear as a glowing overlay, it casts real moving light and real shadows on every surface, and it produces real dark smoke. Real phone-camera look with slight sensor noise and natural motion blur only from the handheld movement. No text overlays, no captions, no watermark, no on-screen graphics.

POSITIVE LOCKS: one continuous take from 0.0s to 5.0s with no cut anywhere. Everything plays at real-time speed. The camera never zooms; the only camera movement in the entire clip is the backwards retreat that begins at 0.8s. Before 0.8s the camera holds its start-frame position. Nobody stops moving once the fire starts.
```

**Output** - `clips/v9_c01_g5.mp4`, 5711448 bytes, md5
`cd764e28321b156ec8668c2d381fcd8a`, 1080x1920, HEVC, 24 fps, 121 frames,
duration 5.05s, AAC audio.

The clip is `g5` and not `g8`. Take numbers are per shot **per kind**
(`_tool/NAMING.md` section 4): frames and clips number independently. C01
already holds `v9_c01_g1.mp4` through `g4.mp4` from the abandoned end-cap
concept, so the first clip of the remake is `g5` even though it was animated off
frame `g8`. Which frame it came from is recorded here, never in the filename.

### What came back - verified with ffmpeg, frame by frame

Scene detection at threshold 0.35 returns **zero cuts** - it is one continuous
take, as asked. A labelled contact sheet at 0.6 / 0.7 / 0.75 / 0.8 / 0.85 / 0.9
/ 1.0 / 1.2s and again across 1.4 to 5.0s gives the real beat timings:

- the plug goes in and the bulbs on the knot and the tree flash on at **~1.0s**
- the fire is first visible in the tree at **~1.4s**, climbing hard by 1.6s
- the camera starts retreating at **~2.3s**
- all three are running down the hallway at the lens from ~3.0s to the end,
  large in frame at 5.0s with the burning tree small and bright behind them
- no phone, hand or arm anywhere in frame at any point
- an RMS scan of the audio track shows a small spike at 0.9-1.0s, then the fire
  and the screaming from 1.4s onward; the spoken line cannot be confirmed from a
  waveform, only by listening

**The defect to fix on a re-roll: the ignition is ~0.4s late.** The prompt
demanded the flame on the same frame as the plug and it still came out as
plug-then-flash-then-fire. The fused-instant sentence was not enough on its own
here. The next attempt should keep this prompt byte-identical and change only
the ignition line - move the whole event earlier (state it at 0.4s, not 0.8s) so
that even with the model's habitual lag the fire lands where Daniel wants it,
and delete the phrase "flashes on for a split second", which is almost certainly
what bought the model the extra beat between the plug and the flame.

### Video prompt - clips/v9_c01_g6.mp4

Reused from `clips/v9_c01_g5.mp4` (this same shot) - changed: **the cameraman's
retreat only, cut from a full backwards walk down the hallway to two or three
short steps.** Daniel on 2026-09-20 after seeing g5:

> The person filming must make just a few steps back not run the entire hall. In
> rest is good

"In rest is good" means everything else stays byte-identical, including the late
ignition noted under g5. Only the two camera-retreat sentences and the POSITIVE
LOCKS camera line were edited, and a BANNED clause was added. The 0.0-0.8s beat,
the fused ignition instant, the AUDIO block and the REALISM rules are untouched
from g5.

**Start frame:** `starting_frames/v9_c01_g8.png` - Higgsfield media
`76e29118-dbc9-4087-b4c7-7c786041615f`, role `start_image` inside `medias`.

`count 1`, `use_unlim false`,
`declined_preset_id 24bae836-2c4a-48e0-89b6-49fcc0b21612`.
Job `be1f5aac-9f99-4dd8-bdab-d20b8f9607bf`. Accepted first try.

```
Single continuous handheld iPhone shot, 5 seconds, vertical 9:16, hyperrealistic. No cuts, no zoom, no slow motion, no speed ramp. The camera itself IS the phone being held by a man standing in the hallway doorway, so we see ONLY what he sees. No phone, no screen, no hand, no fingers, no thumb, no arm, no sleeve, no device and no object ever appears in the foreground or along any edge of the frame.

Start frame: a blonde mother in a forest-green cable-knit sweater kneels on pale wooden floorboards in the lower left, her right arm stretched out to a white wall socket on the white corner at the right of the frame, holding the plug of a big tangled knot of unlit multicoloured Christmas string lights that lies on the floor beside her. One continuous run of that same string leads away across the floor and up into the decorated but unlit Christmas tree standing on the right. A boy in red-and-white striped pyjamas and a small girl in a cream nightgown stand behind her near the navy sofa.

ACTION TIMELINE:
0.0-0.8s - The mother pushes the plug the last few centimetres into the socket. Her hand moves slowly and steadily, her head still turned in profile watching it. Everything else in the room is calm and still. The tree is dark. The camera is locked where it stands in the start frame, only natural handheld sway.

AT 0.8s - ONE SINGLE INSTANT, AND EVERYTHING IN IT HAPPENS ON THE SAME FRAME. The two pins seat in the socket; every bulb on the knot and on the tree flashes on for a split second; and in that same instant a burst of orange flame erupts low in the Christmas tree where the string meets the branches and the tree catches fire. On that exact same frame the man holding the camera shouts "Oh, shoot" and the whole frame jolts as he flinches backwards. The fire is not delayed, it does not build up, it does not start small and grow for a second first - the tree is already burning on the same frame the plug goes in.

0.8-2.0s - The flames climb the tree fast and realistically, running up through the dry branches, orange and yellow, throwing real flickering firelight across the shiplap walls, the floorboards, the sofa and the three faces, with dark smoke rolling up off the top of the tree toward the ceiling. Ornaments glow and the branches blacken. The mother recoils hard away from the socket, twists round onto her hands and pushes herself up off the floor. The boy and the girl scream and turn toward the camera. The man with the camera takes ONLY TWO OR THREE SHORT STEPS BACKWARDS and then stops and stands his ground. That small retreat is finished by about 1.6s. He does not walk backwards down the hallway, he does not run, he does not retreat any further at any later point in the clip.

2.0-5.0s - The camera is now STANDING STILL in the hallway just behind the doorway, holding its position with only natural handheld sway, not moving backwards at all. The mother is on her feet and runs straight toward the camera with her arms out, scooping the two children ahead of her, all three sprinting toward the lens and growing rapidly larger in the frame, faces wide with panic, the boy's arms pumping, the little girl half carried. Because the camera is no longer backing away, they close the distance fast and come right up to the lens. End frame: the three of them very close and very large in the frame mid-run, almost filling it, the burning tree bright behind them through the doorway.

AUDIO: natural room sound only. One male voice, off camera behind the lens, shouting "Oh, shoot" exactly on the frame the tree catches fire - nothing else from him before it, nothing else after it. Then the crackle and roar of the fire, the mother shouting wordlessly, the two children screaming, bare feet running on wood. NO music, no score, no background song, no narration, no added sound effects beyond these.

REALISM - HARD RULES: the mother, the boy and the girl keep exactly the same faces, hair, clothes and body shapes as the start frame throughout. The room stays the same room - same cream shiplap walls, same navy sofa with the mustard blanket, same pale floorboards, same rug, same lamp, same window. The fire behaves like real fire on a real dry conifer: it spreads along branches, it does not float, it does not appear as a glowing overlay, it casts real moving light and real shadows on every surface, and it produces real dark smoke. Real phone-camera look with slight sensor noise and natural motion blur only from the handheld movement. No text overlays, no captions, no watermark, no on-screen graphics.

POSITIVE LOCKS: one continuous take from 0.0s to 5.0s with no cut anywhere. Everything plays at real-time speed. The camera never zooms. The ONLY camera movement in the entire clip is a short backwards flinch of two or three steps between 0.8s and 1.6s; before 0.8s the camera holds its start-frame position and after 1.6s the camera holds its new position and stays there until the end. BANNED: the camera walking or running backwards down the hallway, the camera retreating for more than three steps, the camera moving away from the family during their run, the family staying the same size in the frame as they run. Nobody stops moving once the fire starts.
```

**Output** - `clips/v9_c01_g6.mp4`, 6039365 bytes, md5
`e7c8c04cc95b5cbae62f3629a921e3ba`, 1080x1920, HEVC, 24 fps, 121 frames,
duration 5.05s, AAC audio.

**Verified with ffmpeg.** Scene detection at 0.35 returns zero cuts. The
labelled contact sheet at 0.6 / 0.9 / 1.2 / 1.5 / 1.8 / 2.2 / 2.6 / 3.0 / 3.4 /
3.8 / 4.2 / 4.6s shows the camera holding the doorway framing all the way
through - the white door jamb stays at the same place in the frame and the
hallway never opens up behind it, so the long backwards walk is gone. The bulbs
come on at ~1.5s, the tree is alight from ~1.8s, and from ~3.4s the mother and
both children run at the lens and fill the frame by 4.6-5.0s, larger than they
ever got in g5 because the camera no longer backs off.

**The lesson.** The fix that worked was not more adjectives about a "small"
retreat - it was giving the retreat an explicit end time ("finished by about
1.6s"), stating what the camera does for the rest of the clip ("STANDING STILL
... not moving backwards at all"), and adding the failure mode itself to BANNED
("the camera walking or running backwards down the hallway"). A camera move
needs a stop time the same way an action beat needs a start time.

The late ignition from g5 is still present here (~1.5s flash, ~1.8s fire) and was
deliberately left alone on Daniel's instruction. If it is ever fixed, the edit is
already written up at the end of the g5 entry above.

## Upscale round - 2026-09-21 - all seven clips to 2K

Every picked clip in this video was upscaled with **ByteDance Upscale**, driven in
the browser at `https://higgsfield.ai/upscale` because **Model version: Pro is
UI-only** - the MCP `upscale_video` tool rejects `model_version` outright
(`additionalProperties: false`), so Pro cannot be reached from the API.

Settings, identical on all seven except the preset:

```
ByteDance Upscale
Model version: Pro
Resolution:    2K
FPS:           30 fps
Preset:        AIGC   for C01
               Common for C02-C07
```

The upscale carries its source clip's exact filename and lives in
`clips/upscaled/` - the folder is the suffix, per `_tool/NAMING.md`.

| shot | source clip | preset | upscale | upscale job | source | result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C01 | `clips/v9_c01_g6.mp4` | **AIGC** | `clips/upscaled/v9_c01_g6.mp4` | `0063383a` | 1080x1920 · 24 fps · 5.05 s · 5.8 MB | 1440x2560 · 30 fps · 5.06 s · 10.5 MB |
| C02 | `clips/v9_c02_g1.mp4` | Common | `clips/upscaled/v9_c02_g1.mp4` | `9cfca776` | 720x1280 · 24 fps · 4.05 s · 1.7 MB | 1440x2560 · 30 fps · 4.07 s · 8.5 MB |
| C03 | `clips/v9_c03_g1.mp4` | Common | `clips/upscaled/v9_c03_g1.mp4` | `86ad2840` | 720x1280 · 24 fps · 4.05 s · 1.5 MB | 1440x2560 · 30 fps · 4.07 s · 7.8 MB |
| C04 | `clips/v9_c04_g1.mp4` | Common | `clips/upscaled/v9_c04_g1.mp4` | `07856168` | 720x1280 · 24 fps · 4.05 s · 1.1 MB | 1440x2560 · 30 fps · 4.07 s · 5.9 MB |
| C05 | `clips/v9_c05_g3.mp4` | Common | `clips/upscaled/v9_c05_g3.mp4` | `c7500d48` | 720x1280 · 24 fps · 5.05 s · 3.2 MB | 1440x2560 · 30 fps · 5.06 s · 9.8 MB |
| C06 | `clips/v9_c06_g2.mp4` | Common | `clips/upscaled/v9_c06_g2.mp4` | `a7dde691` | 720x1280 · 24 fps · 5.05 s · 4.0 MB | 1440x2560 · 30 fps · 5.06 s · 10.2 MB |
| C07 | `clips/v9_c07_g1.mp4` | Common | `clips/upscaled/v9_c07_g1.mp4` | `6a1e488c` | 720x1280 · 24 fps · 4.05 s · 3.2 MB | 1440x2560 · 30 fps · 4.07 s · 8.7 MB |

In every case the **last** take of the shot was the one upscaled - C05 on `g3`,
C06 on `g2`, C01 on `g6`, the rest on `g1`. Cost was 1.62 credits per job,
billed to the **Oneofone1** workspace.

### Result URLs

```
C01  https://d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net/user_REMOVED/hf_20260920_220127_7c389dee-3ee3-4da5-adcb-2763bbd58080.mp4
C02  https://d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net/user_REMOVED/hf_20260920_220232_e6a0cae3-0fa4-4a4d-b4ae-145c724fce2d.mp4
C03  https://d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net/user_REMOVED/hf_20260920_220336_be5e4f18-0447-4644-afa2-6bca69785486.mp4
C04  https://d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net/user_REMOVED/hf_20260920_220431_e65b44a3-6e0b-40c8-b742-569e1a25e1da.mp4
C05  https://d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net/user_REMOVED/hf_20260920_220514_80dc3751-2e28-49a3-ae78-cf14dff06bea.mp4
C06  https://d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net/user_REMOVED/hf_20260920_220554_da346adf-abcf-46a6-9954-1840cfe7c4c9.mp4
C07  https://d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net/user_REMOVED/hf_20260920_220634_21e1aef3-0b42-42e9-8175-ed544c85f431.mp4
```

### What was learned

**The preset is sticky across navigations.** After C01 went through on AIGC, a
freshly loaded `/upscale` still read AIGC for the next clip. A preset left
unchecked silently applies the wrong model to the whole rest of a batch, so
every submit in this round went through a guard that read the panel back and
refused to click Upscale unless it said exactly `Pro + 2K + 30fps` and the
intended preset.

**A submit can silently no-op.** The only reliable proof a job was created is
`location.href` flipping from `?media=video--<id>` to `?media=video-job--<id>`.
All fourteen submits in this round were verified that way.

**Done-ness is a hostname, not a video element.** While a job renders, the page
still shows a `<video>` - pointing at the *source* on `d2ol7oe51mr4n9`. It flips
to `d8j0ntlcm91z4` when the upscale lands.

**Every download was byte-checked** against the remote `Content-Length`. One
(`v10_c03_g1`) came down truncated at 1,703,936 of 7,545,142 bytes and was
re-fetched - a silent short read that a casual eyeball would have missed.

#### Image prompt — `starting_frames/v9_c01_g8.png` — IN THE CUT

**Settings** - gpt_image_2_5 · sunburst · 9:16 · 2k · medium · 1520×2688

**Result** - https://d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net/user_REMOVED/hf_20260920_193304_443746c4-8eeb-4ff2-a4da-e02a14293714.png
**References, in submission order**

````text
Extremely high quality candid vertical iPhone photo taken from a hallway looking into a family's living room on a winter evening: a young mother kneels on the bare floor with a huge tangled knot of old multicoloured Christmas string lights in front of her, her arm stretched out to push the plug into a wall socket, while her two small children stand behind her watching the Christmas tree.

Use the attached reference photograph for BOTH the staging AND the camera position - the mother kneeling in the near foreground, the knot of lights on the floor beside her, her arm reaching out to the low wall socket, the two small children standing behind her, the tall Christmas tree on the right and the plain white wall corner the camera is looking past. COPY ITS CAMERA POSITION AND ITS COMPOSITION EXACTLY. Copy nothing else from it: the room, the mother and the children are all described below and are all different people in a different house.

FRAMING AND ANGLE - COPY THE CAMERA FROM THE REFERENCE: the camera is handheld at about the chest height of a standing adult, tilted very slightly down, standing in the next room and looking in through a doorway, so a plain white wall corner with a white skirting board runs floor to ceiling down the RIGHT EDGE of the frame and takes up roughly the right eighth of the picture - we are looking past it into the living room. The living room floor fills the bottom half of the frame and runs away into depth. The mother kneels in the lower LEFT of the frame, seen from behind at three quarters, close to the camera and large in the picture: her head and shoulders sit around the middle of the frame and the bottom edge of the picture cuts her just below the knees. The knot of lights lies on the bare floor immediately to her right, between her and that white wall corner, around the centre of the lower third of the picture. The Christmas tree stands on the RIGHT of the frame just inside the white corner, tall enough that the top edge of the picture cuts off its top. The two children stand on the floor in the middle distance between the mother and the tree, small in the frame, their heads in the middle third of the picture. Nobody is looking at the camera and nobody is posing. This is NOT a side angle, NOT a low floor-level angle and NOT a symmetrical composition.

THE KNOT AND THE PLUG: on the bare floorboards immediately to the mother's right lies a big ugly knot of old-fashioned Christmas string lights - dark green flex wound and knotted into a messy nest about the width of two floorboards across, studded with fat vintage multicoloured bulbs in red, green, blue and yellow, with a white in-line plug body and loops of slack wire lifting out of the top of it. She holds the end of that flex in her outstretched right hand and the two-pin plug is an inch from a white electrical socket set low on the white wall corner at the right of the frame. It is not plugged in yet and every bulb in the knot is dark.

THE CONNECTION - THIS IS THE IMPORTANT PART: one single continuous run of that SAME dark green flex with the SAME fat multicoloured bulbs leaves the far side of the knot, travels away across the floorboards to the right, passes behind the children, reaches the foot of the Christmas tree and climbs up into its lower branches, where it is plainly the very same string that is wound round and round the whole tree above. Same wire colour, same bulb size, same bulb spacing, one unbroken line. The eye must be able to follow the wire from the plug in her hand, through the knot on the floor, across the floor and up onto the tree without a single break or gap. The heap on the floor is NOT a separate loose pile with nothing leading out of it, and the tree is NOT wired to anything else.

THE TREE IS NOT SWITCHED ON: because the plug is still in her hand, every bulb on the tree is dark and unlit. The tree is not glowing, no light comes off it, and it throws no coloured light onto the floor, the walls or the children. It is still fully decorated and still reads as a beautiful Christmas tree - the baubles catch the warm lamplight in the room - it is simply not lit yet.

THE ROOM: a cosy American farmhouse living room. Walls of cream-white painted shiplap boards with white skirting, wide pale grey-washed pine floorboards running away from the camera, a deep navy-blue slipcovered sofa along the left wall with a mustard wool blanket folded over its back, a cream and charcoal flat-weave rug on the floor in front of it, an oak side table behind the sofa carrying a black ceramic table lamp with a white linen shade that is switched on and glowing, and a white-painted window with black muntins on the back wall showing a dark blue winter evening and snow on the sill outside.

THE TREE: a big real fir, dense and dark green, dressed with silver, white and pale blue glass baubles, cream knitted snowflakes, a wide cream linen ribbon wound down it and a silver star on top, with a heavy cream cable-knit tree skirt gathered round its base and four presents wrapped in white paper and green ribbon sitting on it.

THE MOTHER: about 28, long wavy honey-blonde hair falling loose past her shoulders, an oversized forest-green chunky cable-knit sweater with the sleeves pushed up her forearms, charcoal grey joggers and thick oatmeal socks, no shoes. She kneels sitting back on her heels, her left hand flat on the floorboards taking her weight, her right arm stretched right out towards the socket with the plug pinched between her thumb and fingers, her head turned in profile watching the plug. She is concentrating, slightly fed up, not smiling.

THE CHILDREN: a boy of about seven standing on the rug behind his mother, straight light-brown hair, red-and-white striped pyjamas, barefoot, his mouth wide open in a huge exaggerated gasp of amazement and both hands up near his cheeks, staring at the tree. Beside him a girl of about four with blonde hair in two short pigtails, a cream flannel nightgown printed with tiny red hearts, crouched down on one knee with her right arm flung out pointing straight at the tree. Neither child blocks the mother, the knot of lights or the tree.

COLOUR: warm domestic evening white balance, the way an iPhone renders a lamp-lit living room - warm but not orange. The picture is NOT sepia, NOT amber-washed and NOT heavily graded. Skin reads natural, the white wall corner reads clean white, the floorboards read as real wood and the sweater reads as true forest green.

LIGHTING AND QUALITY: the room is lit by the warm table lamp plus soft ambient light - cosy and warm, but still bright enough that every face, every twist of wire in the knot and the whole tree are clearly readable, with no dark corners where detail disappears and no blown highlights. Shot on an iPhone 16 Pro and straight out of the camera roll - an ordinary everyday snapshot somebody took of their family, not a magazine photo and not a rendered image. Extremely high quality, tack sharp and in focus everywhere from the mother in the foreground to the tree behind her, crisp detail in every individual bulb and every twist of flex in the tangle, realistic skin, hair and knitwear texture. Absolutely nothing blurry: no motion blur, no soft focus, no background blur, no bokeh, no grain, no film look, no cinematic grade, no vignette. Normal wide-angle lens, vertical lines vertical and parallel, no fisheye distortion.

FRAMING: leave the upper LEFT of the image - roughly the top eighth of the frame on the wall side - as plain uncluttered wall, free of pictures, shelves, garland, hanging decorations and lettering, so a caption can sit there. Do not pull the camera back or shrink the mother to make that room.

No captions, no watermarks, no added lettering anywhere in the image, no writing on any clothing, no user-interface elements, no mute or speaker icons and no social-media overlays.
````

**Result** - https://d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net/user_REMOVED/hf_20260920_210433_be1f5aac-9f99-4dd8-bdab-d20b8f9607bf.mp4 (byte-identical to the local file, md5 checked 2026-09-23)
**Upscaled derivative** - `clips/upscaled/v9_c01_g6.mp4` (1440×2560)
**Attached, in submission order**
- start frame: `content/video_9_assets/starting_frames/v9_c01_g8.png` · Higgsfield media `76e29118-dbc9-4087-b4c7-7c786041615f`

````text
Single continuous handheld iPhone shot, 5 seconds, vertical 9:16, hyperrealistic. No cuts, no zoom, no slow motion, no speed ramp. The camera itself IS the phone being held by a man standing in the hallway doorway, so we see ONLY what he sees. No phone, no screen, no hand, no fingers, no thumb, no arm, no sleeve, no device and no object ever appears in the foreground or along any edge of the frame.

Start frame: a blonde mother in a forest-green cable-knit sweater kneels on pale wooden floorboards in the lower left, her right arm stretched out to a white wall socket on the white corner at the right of the frame, holding the plug of a big tangled knot of unlit multicoloured Christmas string lights that lies on the floor beside her. One continuous run of that same string leads away across the floor and up into the decorated but unlit Christmas tree standing on the right. A boy in red-and-white striped pyjamas and a small girl in a cream nightgown stand behind her near the navy sofa.

ACTION TIMELINE:
0.0-0.8s - The mother pushes the plug the last few centimetres into the socket. Her hand moves slowly and steadily, her head still turned in profile watching it. Everything else in the room is calm and still. The tree is dark. The camera is locked where it stands in the start frame, only natural handheld sway.

AT 0.8s - ONE SINGLE INSTANT, AND EVERYTHING IN IT HAPPENS ON THE SAME FRAME. The two pins seat in the socket; every bulb on the knot and on the tree flashes on for a split second; and in that same instant a burst of orange flame erupts low in the Christmas tree where the string meets the branches and the tree catches fire. On that exact same frame the man holding the camera shouts "Oh, shoot" and the whole frame jolts as he flinches backwards. The fire is not delayed, it does not build up, it does not start small and grow for a second first - the tree is already burning on the same frame the plug goes in.

0.8-2.0s - The flames climb the tree fast and realistically, running up through the dry branches, orange and yellow, throwing real flickering firelight across the shiplap walls, the floorboards, the sofa and the three faces, with dark smoke rolling up off the top of the tree toward the ceiling. Ornaments glow and the branches blacken. The mother recoils hard away from the socket, twists round onto her hands and pushes herself up off the floor. The boy and the girl scream and turn toward the camera. The man with the camera takes ONLY TWO OR THREE SHORT STEPS BACKWARDS and then stops and stands his ground. That small retreat is finished by about 1.6s. He does not walk backwards down the hallway, he does not run, he does not retreat any further at any later point in the clip.

2.0-5.0s - The camera is now STANDING STILL in the hallway just behind the doorway, holding its position with only natural handheld sway, not moving backwards at all. The mother is on her feet and runs straight toward the camera with her arms out, scooping the two children ahead of her, all three sprinting toward the lens and growing rapidly larger in the frame, faces wide with panic, the boy's arms pumping, the little girl half carried. Because the camera is no longer backing away, they close the distance fast and come right up to the lens. End frame: the three of them very close and very large in the frame mid-run, almost filling it, the burning tree bright behind them through the doorway.

AUDIO: natural room sound only. One male voice, off camera behind the lens, shouting "Oh, shoot" exactly on the frame the tree catches fire - nothing else from him before it, nothing else after it. Then the crackle and roar of the fire, the mother shouting wordlessly, the two children screaming, bare feet running on wood. NO music, no score, no background song, no narration, no added sound effects beyond these.

REALISM - HARD RULES: the mother, the boy and the girl keep exactly the same faces, hair, clothes and body shapes as the start frame throughout. The room stays the same room - same cream shiplap walls, same navy sofa with the mustard blanket, same pale floorboards, same rug, same lamp, same window. The fire behaves like real fire on a real dry conifer: it spreads along branches, it does not float, it does not appear as a glowing overlay, it casts real moving light and real shadows on every surface, and it produces real dark smoke. Real phone-camera look with slight sensor noise and natural motion blur only from the handheld movement. No text overlays, no captions, no watermark, no on-screen graphics.

POSITIVE LOCKS: one continuous take from 0.0s to 5.0s with no cut anywhere. Everything plays at real-time speed. The camera never zooms. The ONLY camera movement in the entire clip is a short backwards flinch of two or three steps between 0.8s and 1.6s; before 0.8s the camera holds its start-frame position and after 1.6s the camera holds its new position and stays there until the end. BANNED: the camera walking or running backwards down the hallway, the camera retreating for more than three steps, the camera moving away from the family during their run, the family staying the same size in the frame as they run. Nobody stops moving once the fire starts.
````

THE TREE: a big real spruce, dense and dark green, dressed with red and gold glass baubles, small cream wooden stars, a red tartan ribbon wound down it and a gold star on top, with a thick red velvet tree skirt pooled on the floor at its base and three presents wrapped in kraft paper and red twine sitting on it.

THE MOTHER: about 32, dark chestnut brown hair falling loose to the middle of her back with a few strands across her face, an oversized cream Fair Isle sweater with bands of red and forest green snowflakes across the chest and sleeves, dark indigo jeans and thick cream ribbed socks, no shoes. She kneels sitting back on her heels, her left hand flat on the floorboards taking her weight, her right arm stretched right out towards the socket with the plug pinched between her thumb and fingers, her head turned in profile watching the plug. She is concentrating, slightly fed up, not smiling.

THE CHILDREN: a girl of about six standing on the rug behind her mother, dark curly hair in two high puffs tied with red ribbon, a red tartan flannel pyjama set, barefoot, her mouth wide open in a huge exaggerated gasp of amazement and both hands up near her cheeks, staring at the tree. Beside her a boy of about four with straight black hair cut in a fringe, green pyjamas printed with small reindeer, crouched down on one knee with his right arm flung out pointing straight at the tree. Neither child blocks the mother, the knot of lights or the tree.

COLOUR: warm domestic evening white balance, the way an iPhone renders a lamp-lit living room - warm but not orange. The picture is NOT sepia, NOT amber-washed and NOT heavily graded. Skin reads natural, the white wall corner reads clean white, the floorboards read as real wood and the sweater reads as true cream.

LIGHTING AND QUALITY: the room is lit by the warm table lamp plus soft ambient light - cosy and warm, but still bright enough that every face, every twist of wire in the knot and the whole tree are clearly readable, with no dark corners where detail disappears and no blown highlights. Shot on an iPhone 16 Pro and straight out of the camera roll - an ordinary everyday snapshot somebody took of their family, not a magazine photo and not a rendered image. Extremely high quality, tack sharp and in focus everywhere from the mother in the foreground to the tree behind her, crisp detail in every individual bulb and every twist of flex in the tangle, realistic skin, hair and knitwear texture. Absolutely nothing blurry: no motion blur, no soft focus, no background blur, no bokeh, no grain, no film look, no cinematic grade, no vignette. AND EVERY PIECE OF STANDING-PROUD TIMBER AND TRIM THROWS A REAL SHADOW: each chunky beam and each raised batten casts a hard-edged shadow of itself across the surface behind it, so a dark unlit band runs alongside every beam and every batten where the picture is blocked.
