````text
HIGHEST PRIORITY, READ THIS FIRST: the pictures on the walls and the vaulted ceiling are A MOVIE THAT IS PLAYING, not a pattern painted on the drywall. @Image 1 is ONE PAUSED FRAME of that movie. At 0.0s it un-pauses and keeps running to the last frame. Steam pours off the train, the aurora ripples, the sleigh flies, the great spiral winds, the floating gifts drift and turn, the snow falls - continuously, in every single frame. If the shapes on the walls and ceiling hold still, the clip has failed.

Wholesome family Christmas scene, children at home at Christmas.

TASK: image-to-video, one continuous handheld phone POV take, vertical 9:16. Frame 0.0s is the start image: a closed honey-stained pine plank-and-batten door with a wreath, a hand on the brass knob. @Image 1 is the great room behind that door and the authority on its layout, rafters, furniture, the two children, their clothes, the colours, and the exact set and style of every projected shape.

THE SIX MOVING THINGS - all six running from the first frame to the last.

THE STEAM. The long lit steam train running out of the pines pours steam like a real locomotive working on a cold still evening: it boils out of the funnel, leans back over the carriage roofs, climbs and widens and thins away, and the next plume is already forming behind it, with gold sparks lifting off it the whole time.

THE AURORA. The crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbons ripple like a long silk curtain hung in a draught - a slow swell travels the length of each ribbon, brightening it as it passes and combing its lower fringe into new folds. The arc stays where it is on the drywall; the swell inside it travels, and the ribbons that feed into the mouth of the great spiral keep streaming inward along their whole length.

THE SLEIGH. Santa's sleigh and its eight reindeer are FLYING ACROSS THE CEILING the way a plane crosses the sky, sweeping out of the mouth of the great spiral and away across the slopes. Legs gallop, heads nod, the sleigh rocks, the long trail of light streams off the runners back into the spiral, the team rises and dips. They start near one side of the vault and finish far across it. Never parked.

THE GREAT SPIRAL. The colossal whirlpool of light filling the vault is WINDING: all seven concentric rings turn steadily about the brilliant white-gold core on the ridge beam, each ring creeping inward and tightening as it goes, the countless tiny points of light inside them streaming along the curve like a slow river of stars circling a drain. The spiral never sits still and its outer boundary never slides off the ceiling.

THE FLOATING GIFTS. Each glowing wrapped gift DRIFTS through the scene at its own depth and its own speed, and every one of them is turning slowly end over end as it goes, so the ribbon cross and the big soft bow swing round into view and away again.

THE SNOW. The big soft six-point flakes and the tiny points of light fall the way heavy snow falls past a window at night - down and slightly sideways, each turning on its own centre. One fades out at the floor line as a fresh one fades in high near the ridge, so the density never thins.

Everything else travels too: gold sparks lifting off the locomotive, shooting stars streaking and fading, the lit gold windows of the fairy-tale city and of the train's carriages flickering warm, wisps of crimson and gold cloud drifting between the aurora bands, and the tiny black silhouette of the second sleigh crawling on across the face of the moon. Only the pale blue moon holds position; it simply breathes brighter and dimmer.

ONE PROJECTOR, ONE PICTURE: whatever the wall is doing, the vault is doing at the same instant and the same speed. The picture's outer boundary is anchored - it never rotates, spins or slides across the room as one sheet; only the things inside it travel. It is flat light lying on the surfaces: it skews with their slope, bends over the rafters and breaks where wall meets vault. Nothing ever leaves a surface, and nothing is projected on the floor, rug, sofa, table, tree or gifts.

THE VAULT IS FULL. The ceiling carries as much moving picture as the walls do, everywhere it appears in frame and above all in the closing seconds: the winding spiral, the aurora, the flying sleigh, drifting gifts, pouring snow and twinkling stars run right up to the ridge and into both corners. No bare un-animated patch of warm-white drywall anywhere in the closing framing.

BEATS
0.0-0.4s - The hand turns the knob and shoves the door away from camera in ONE fast decisive push. THE DOOR IS WIDE OPEN AND THE WHOLE ROOM IS VISIBLE BY 0.4s, and the hand and the door are gone from the frame from 0.4s onward - this is a half-second beat, not a slow reveal. The room behind it is already running AND BOTH CHILDREN ARE ALREADY IN MOTION - the widening gap only uncovers more of a movie that was already playing and two kids who were already running before the door opened. Nothing ignites or spreads as the door opens, and the door edge and frame stay plain painted wood - no glowing strip, no coloured fringe, no bright seam in the gap.
0.4s to the end - ONE SINGLE UNBROKEN CAMERA MOVE, exactly like a person walking into a room with their chin lifting the whole way. The camera walks steadily forward while its aim rises smoothly at a constant rate, so the frame climbs from the floor to the dark walnut rafters and the warm-white vault in one continuous sweep. Walk and rise happen together at the same steady pace for the whole span; the camera never pauses, never holds and never re-starts, and there is no second move.
Inside that single move, BOTH CHILDREN ARE ALREADY RUNNING THE FIRST FRAME THEY ARE VISIBLE. The door opens on two kids in the middle of doing something, not on two kids standing waiting to be filmed, and neither of them is ever still. THE BOY, the smaller child nearer the camera, is already seen from behind with his back to the lens and pushing off his front foot as the gap widens; he keeps running away from camera, on past the big light-grey sectional sofa on the left and deeper into the room toward the lit tree, never once turning front-on to the lens and still running on the last frame. THE GIRL, the taller child with the long dark hair, is already mid-stride deeper in the room as the door opens; she runs on across the rug toward the fireplace without breaking step, tips her head right back to look straight up into the vault and throws one arm up pointing at the great winding spiral overhead, mouth open, delighted - and she is still moving and still pointing on the last frame. Both runs are ordinary excited-kid running at real speed with real weight: feet landing, knees bending, arms swinging, hair and pyjamas moving with them. Projected shapes slide down the walls behind them and pass across their backs.

CAMERA - eye height, normal wide-angle phone lens, natural handheld bounce. One move for the whole clip. No stop, no second move, no cut, no zoom, no orbit, no fisheye, no curved walls. The camera is the person's eyes; no phone, screen or device in frame after 0.4s.

EXACTLY TWO CHILDREN, no adults, no third child, no pets. Both children stay anatomically correct and consistent from first frame to last: normal child proportions, correct limb lengths and joints, five fingers per hand, faces that keep the same features and never smear, stretch, melt or change. Hold the sharpness and exposure of the start image - the plank and batten edges of the honey-stained pine door, the brass knob, the bare forearm and its thin gold bangle. Crisp phone footage, in focus first frame to last.

AUDIO - dry documentary sound only: the handle, the door swinging, running feet, children laughing, quiet room tone. (no BGM). No subtitles.

POSITIVE LOCKS - real-time motion at normal speed. One continuous take. One single smooth camera move. The projected picture is visibly in motion in every single frame, at the start, in the middle and in the last second. The vault is covered in moving picture at the end. BOTH CHILDREN ARE MOVING IN EVERY FRAME THEY APPEAR IN, from the first frame the door uncovers them to the last frame of the clip: the boy ends the clip still running away from camera deeper into the room, the girl ends it still running and still pointing up at the ceiling. Their movement is natural, unchoreographed, real-speed kid movement. Sharp focus to the final frame.

BANNED - a still or frozen picture; a projection that appears to move only because the camera moves; light that moves for a moment then stops; a locomotive without a moving steam plume; an aurora that sits still; a parked sleigh; a spiral whose rings do not wind; a floating gift that does not drift and turn; flakes that twinkle but never travel; a still ceiling above a moving wall; an empty or un-animated area of ceiling in any frame; the whole picture rotating or sliding as one sheet; a pattern that looks printed, painted or like wallpaper; a glow or bright seam along the door edge or gap; light that grows or fills the room as the door opens; a door that is still opening after 0.4s; a hand still on the knob after 0.4s; a slow or drawn-out door reveal; particles falling through the open air of the room; volumetric snow; snow in front of the children, the sofa, the stair or the real tree; a camera that moves, stops and then moves again; two separate camera moves; a child facing the lens front-on; a child climbing, jumping or landing on the sofa; either child standing still, waiting, hesitating, posed or rooted to one spot at any point; a child who only turns her head while her feet stay planted; a child who begins the clip motionless and starts moving later; a child who stops moving before the last frame; a distorted, smeared, melted or warped child's face; warped or extra limbs; wrong number of fingers; the projector unit visible; slow motion; blur; any music; a cut; an adult; subtitles; on-screen text.

ACROSS BOTH CEILING SLOPES OVERHEAD, one colossal spiral whirlpool of light filling the whole vault with its centre sitting right on the ridge beam, so half of it lies on one slope and half on the other and every ring visibly bends where it crosses the ridge - seven concentric glowing rings of deep crimson, ruby red, warm gold and emerald green winding in on each other in a slow tightening spiral and drawing down to one brilliant white-gold core at the very centre, every ring built of countless tiny points of light so the whole thing reads as a whirlpool of stars rather than a painted disc; a big pale blue full moon glowing high on one of the painted drywall side walls, cool silver-blue and softly cratered and the one cool-coloured thing in the whole picture, with the tiny black silhouette of a second sleigh and its reindeer team crossing right over the face of it; The crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbons ripple like a long silk curtain hung in a draught - a slow swell travels the length of each ribbon, brightening it as it passes and combing its lower fringe into new folds. Legs gallop, heads nod, the sleigh rocks, the long trail of light streams off the runners back into the spiral, the team rises and dips. They start near one side of the vault and finish far across it. Never parked.

THE GREAT SPIRAL. The colossal whirlpool of light filling the vault is WINDING: all seven concentric rings turn steadily about the brilliant white-gold core on the ridge beam, each ring creeping inward and tightening as it goes, the countless tiny points of light inside them streaming along the curve like a slow river of stars circling a drain. POSITIVE LOCKS - real-time motion at normal speed. One continuous take. One single smooth camera move. Nothing ignites or spreads as the door opens, and the door edge and frame stay plain painted wood - no glowing strip, no coloured fringe, no bright seam in the gap.
0.6s to the end - ONE SINGLE UNBROKEN CAMERA MOVE, exactly like a person walking into a room with their chin lifting the whole way. The camera walks steadily forward while its aim rises smoothly at a constant rate, so the frame climbs from the floor to the dark walnut rafters and the warm-white vault in one continuous sweep. Walk and rise happen together at the same steady pace for the whole span; the camera never pauses, never holds and never re-starts, and there is no second move. All through it the steam pours off the train, the aurora ripples, the sleigh flies on across the vault, the great spiral keeps winding, the floating gifts keep drifting and turning and the snow keeps falling.
Inside that single move, BOTH CHILDREN ARE ALREADY RUNNING THE FIRST FRAME THEY ARE VISIBLE. The door opens on two kids in the middle of doing something, not on two kids standing waiting to be filmed, and neither of them is ever still. THE BOY, the smaller child nearer the camera, is already turning away from the lens and pushing off his front foot as the gap widens; he runs three or four quick real steps and LAUNCHES HIMSELF UP ONTO the big light-grey sectional sofa on the left, landing on his knees with both arms flying up, laughing, and keeps bouncing there until the last frame. THE GIRL, the taller child with the long dark hair, is already mid-stride deeper in the room as the door opens; she runs on across the rug toward the fireplace without breaking step, tips her head right back to look straight up into the vault and throws one arm up pointing at the great winding spiral overhead, mouth open, delighted - and she is still moving and still pointing on the last frame. Both runs are ordinary excited-kid running at real speed with real weight: feet landing, knees bending, arms swinging, hair and pyjamas moving with them. Projected shapes slide down the walls behind them and pass across their backs.

CAMERA - eye height, normal wide-angle phone lens, natural handheld bounce. One move for the whole clip. No stop, no second move, no cut, no zoom, no orbit, no fisheye, no curved walls. The camera is the person's eyes; no phone, screen or device in frame after 0.6s.

EXACTLY TWO CHILDREN, no adults, no third child, no pets. Hold the sharpness and exposure of the start image - the plank and batten edges of the honey-stained pine door, the brass knob, the bare forearm and its thin gold bangle. Crisp phone footage, in focus first frame to last.

AUDIO - dry documentary sound only: the handle, the door swinging, running feet, a body landing on cushions, children laughing, quiet room tone. (no BGM). No subtitles.

POSITIVE LOCKS - real-time motion at normal speed. One continuous take. One single smooth camera move. The projected picture is visibly in motion in every single frame, at the start, in the middle and in the last second. The vault is covered in moving picture at the end. BOTH CHILDREN ARE MOVING IN EVERY FRAME THEY APPEAR IN, from the first frame the door uncovers them to the last frame of the clip: the boy ends the clip bouncing on the sofa, the girl ends it still running and still pointing up at the ceiling. Their movement is natural, unchoreographed, real-speed kid movement. Sharp focus to the final frame.

BANNED - a still or frozen picture; a projection that appears to move only because the camera moves; light that moves for a moment then stops; a locomotive without a moving steam plume; an aurora that sits still; a parked sleigh; a spiral whose rings do not wind; a floating gift that does not drift and turn; flakes that twinkle but never travel; a still ceiling above a moving wall; an empty or un-animated area of ceiling in any frame; the whole picture rotating or sliding as one sheet; a pattern that looks printed, painted or like wallpaper; a glow or bright seam along the door edge or gap; light that grows or fills the room as the door opens; particles falling through the open air of the room; volumetric snow; snow in front of the children, the sofa, the stair or the real tree; a camera that moves, stops and then moves again; two separate camera moves; the boy still standing front-on at the end; either child standing still, waiting, hesitating, posed or rooted to one spot at any point; a child who only turns her head while her feet stay planted; a child who begins the clip motionless and starts moving later; a child who stops moving before the last frame; the projector unit visible; slow motion; blur; any music; a cut; an adult; subtitles; on-screen text.

CHANGE 1 - THE CHILD. A different child from @Image 1: a girl of about seven with shoulder-length wavy auburn hair, fully dressed for winter in a warm rust-red and cream nordic knitted Christmas jumper with a row of cream snowflakes across the yoke, matching charcoal knitted lounge trousers and thick oatmeal knitted socks. CHANGE 2 - THE ROOM. The room is the real house photographed in @Image 2. Build its architecture exactly as it is built there, as a real room a real American builder made, and it must read unmistakably as an upstairs bedroom in an ordinary AMERICAN suburban house - a Zillow-listing house in Connecticut, NOT a European attic, NOT an alpine chalet, NOT a barn loft and NOT a rustic cabin: a bedroom under a VAULTED ceiling - two flat sloping ceiling planes rising from low white knee walls on either side and meeting at a single ridge running the whole length of the room away from the camera, both slopes SMOOTH WARM-WHITE PAINTED DRYWALL with no planking and no boards; one heavy dark-walnut-stained ridge beam runs the full length of the apex and five evenly spaced dark-walnut-stained beams run up each slope from the knee wall to that ridge beam like rafters, every beam chunky and square-edged and standing well clear of the drywall behind it, with small round recessed can lights set into the slopes between them; one dormer recess is cut into the left-hand slope, a small box with its own little flat soffit, two cheek walls and one ordinary American double-hung window with white grilles and wide white casing in its face; the low knee walls on both sides are smooth warm-white drywall with white baseboard and white crown moulding where they meet the slope; the far gable wall behind the bed is a full-height white-painted BOARD-AND-BATTEN accent wall, evenly spaced vertical white battens standing proud of the wall from the baseboard all the way up into the gable peak with a narrow white picture-rail shelf running across it, carrying one small double-hung window with wide white casing set high in it; a low white-painted wooden bed with a wide upholstered oatmeal headboard standing centred against that gable wall, two small white nightstands with cream ceramic table lamps, and a woven rush and pale oak chair standing to the right of the bed; a wide light-oak plank floor under a very large pale woven rug. Copy the proportions, the two sloping drywall planes, the ridge beam, the rafter beams and their spacing, the dormer recess, the board-and-batten gable wall and the small gable window from @Image 2 exactly - this is a photograph of a room that exists, and the finished picture must still read as that room.

TWO THINGS CHANGE FROM @Image 2. First, it is night: the windows show a dark blue snow-covered night outside, there is no daylight anywhere in the room, both table lamps and every recessed can light are switched off and dark, and the projection is the only light. Second, the room is decorated for Christmas: the bed is made up with a rust-red wool quilt, a cream cable-knit throw and cream linen pillows, a cream knitted stocking hangs from the white picture-rail shelf above the headboard, a warm fairy-light garland is strung around the gable window, a small lit Christmas tree with rust-red and antique gold ornaments stands on the floor beside the bed, and a small evergreen wreath with a rust-red velvet bow hangs on the board-and-batten wall beside the bed. Nothing else about the room changes.

CHANGE 3 - THE PROJECTION, and this is the part that matters most. It is ONE enormous cinematic Christmas night scene - a single continuous picture, NOT scattered floating icons - and it is far bigger, denser and more beautiful than in @Image 1. It reads as one unbroken field across the white board-and-batten gable wall behind the bed and both sloping drywall ceiling planes above it, from the knee wall on one side, over the ridge beam, and down to the knee wall on the other, wrapping around the corner where wall meets slope without a break, and over every face of every rafter beam and the ridge beam itself and over every raised white batten, with no visible edge, no rectangle and no screen. NO SURFACE IN THE ROOM IS LEFT BARE. Every square inch of wall and ceiling the camera can see is inside the projection, and that explicitly includes the whole gable wall behind the bed from the top of the headboard up to the ridge, every raised white batten on it, both knee walls right down to where they meet the floor, the strips of wall beside and beneath the window, the side wall on the right, and both ceiling slopes all the way into the far corners. Every one of them carries the picture at full density. If any wall or ceiling surface anywhere in the frame reads as plain paint or plain drywall, the image is wrong. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a deep snow-laden pine forest running the whole width of the room along the very bottom of the projection, rank behind rank of little glowing firs in emerald green and warm gold with snow heaped on every branch, and rising straight out of that forest on the left a whole ornate fairy-tale Christmas city built of lit gold - tier upon tier of slender spires, ribbed onion domes, arched galleries and pinnacled towers stacked up the wall, every arched window lit warm gold, fine gold filigree running along every cornice and balustrade, thick snow lying on every ledge and roof and dome, the whole city glowing as though it were carved out of light; and winding out of the pines on the right a long lit steam train, a dark locomotive leading eight carriages whose windows all glow warm gold, a bright plume of steam and sparks trailing back over the carriage roofs and a glowing gold rail curving away behind it through the trees; ABOVE THE FOREST AND THE CITY, wide soft aurora ribbons in deep Christmas crimson and glowing ruby red, with warm gold, emerald green and rose magenta threaded between them and only a thin thread of teal, sweeping up the walls in long slow curves and carrying over onto both sloping ceiling planes, five or six broad overlapping bands layered over each other so there is very little bare surface left between them, each band shot through with a fine glittering grain so it reads as a river of stars rather than a painted stripe, where they lie flat ON the slopes and run up them with the direction of the slope instead of hanging as vertical curtains, visibly kinking and changing direction at the ridge beam where they cross from one slope onto the other, visibly stepping and breaking at every rafter beam they pass under, and visibly rippling across the raised white battens of the gable wall behind the bed, and several of these red and gold ribbons stream inward and feed into the mouth of the great spiral overhead; ACROSS BOTH CEILING SLOPES OVERHEAD, one colossal spiral whirlpool of light filling the whole vault with its centre sitting right on the ridge beam, so half of it lies on one slope and half on the other and every ring visibly bends where it crosses the ridge - seven concentric glowing rings of deep crimson, ruby red, warm gold and emerald green winding in on each other in a slow tightening spiral and drawing down to one brilliant white-gold core at the very centre, every ring built of countless tiny points of light so the whole thing reads as a whirlpool of stars rather than a painted disc; Santa's sleigh drawn by a team of eight flying reindeer sweeping straight out of the mouth of that spiral, seen side-on in glowing warm gold and large enough to read every antler, the harness and bells on each reindeer, the curved runners and Santa himself sitting up in the sleigh with his sack behind him, a long bright tapering trail of golden light streaming back behind the runners into the spiral, the whole team lying flat on one ceiling slope so it is keystoned and stretched by that slope's angle, foreshortening as it recedes along the room and broken into sections by the ridge beam and the rafter beams it passes under; a big pale blue full moon glowing high on the white board-and-batten gable wall above the bed, cool silver-blue and softly cratered and the one cool-coloured thing in the whole picture, with the tiny black silhouette of a second sleigh and its reindeer team crossing right over the face of it; floating wrapped Christmas gifts drifting through the whole scene at five or six different depths and sizes, each a solid glowing box in gold, emerald or rose with a bright ribbon cross and a big soft bow on top, the nearest big enough to read the weave of the ribbon, all of them turning slowly end over end as they drift; a dense field of stars at many different sizes, hundreds of them, from tiny pinpricks to a few large bright ones with soft radiating rays, with four or five long-tailed shooting stars crossing at different angles with bright tapering trails; large soft glowing six-point snowflakes in cream and pale gold at several different sizes, the nearest big enough to read every arm, drifting across both ceiling slopes and both walls; faint wisps of glowing crimson and gold cloud between the aurora bands; and over all of it a heavy fall of hundreds of tiny bright points of light drifting down the walls like real snow caught in a beam. It stays ONE continuous night-sky picture, richly packed and busy, not scattered separate icons. THE PALETTE IS LED BY RED. Deep Christmas crimson and glowing ruby red are the dominant colours of the entire picture, warm gold is second, then emerald green, cream white and rose magenta; soft teal survives only as a thin accent inside one or two of the aurora bands. The aurora ribbons are mostly crimson and ruby with gold and emerald threaded between them, the outer rings of the great spiral are deep red winding in to a gold core, the fairy-tale city glows red-gold, the train's carriage windows burn red-gold, and most of the floating gifts are crimson and gold. Everything is richly saturated, and the single pale blue moon is the only cool note anywhere in it. NO grey, no muddy brown, no violet and no harsh electric blue. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn. The great spiral is an AURORA WHIRLPOOL made of Christmas light with a sleigh flying out of its mouth, not a space nebula: it must NOT look like outer space, a galaxy, a wormhole, a tunnel or anything science-fiction, alien or dystopian - it is a storybook Christmas night sky with a lit gold Christmas city, a steam train, flying reindeer and falling gifts in it. Every element is a separate glowing shape sitting on the surface - nothing is painted, printed, stencilled or wallpapered, and nothing is a flat repeating texture. HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely. The white painted drywall, the white battens and the white trim do NOT read as white anywhere inside the projection field - the light has swallowed their colour. The only things that survive through it are the surface's TEXTURE and its SHADOWS: the slight tooth of the drywall, the joints and edges, the square corners of the beams and battens. That is the ten percent; the colour of the surface is not part of it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white. Contrast is extreme: the cores of the gold city, the train, the spiral and the stars are near-white hot, the mid-tones are deep saturated crimson and emerald, and the empty sky between them is almost black - never pale paint. AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on. Each of the two ceiling slopes is a separate plane at its own angle, and every shape lying on a slope is keystoned and skewed by THAT plane's perspective, so the same aurora band is stretched differently on the left slope than on the right. Every band, every ring of the spiral and every large shape that crosses the ridge visibly FOLDS at the ridge line, changing direction there with a clean kink, exactly like a picture projected across the inside of a tent. Where the light reaches the top of a side wall it breaks again at that corner and runs down the vertical plane at a different angle. Every rafter beam and the heavy ridge beam interrupt it: the face of each beam that points back toward the projector catches its own bright slice of the picture, offset and stepped from the piece on the drywall behind it, while the underside and the far side of every beam sit in hard unlit shadow, so the whole ceiling reads as a lit vault ribbed by dark beams and never as one flat painted mural. The white board-and-batten gable wall breaks it again, every raised batten catching its own bright vertical strip and throwing its own shadow so the picture ripples across that whole wall in a row of ribs, and the picture-rail shelf cuts a hard horizontal shadow line across it. The dormer recess breaks it a third time, its little soffit and its two cheek walls each catching the picture at their own angle so the light steps into the recess and back out of it. The surface texture stays legible inside the light - the tooth of the drywall, the square edges of the beams and the battens, the white crown moulding - so it still reads as projected light on a real room and never as paint, wallpaper, a sticker or a printed panel. AND EVERY PIECE OF STANDING-PROUD TIMBER AND TRIM THROWS A REAL SHADOW: each chunky beam and each raised batten casts a hard-edged shadow of itself across the surface behind it, so a dark unlit band runs alongside every beam and every batten where the picture is blocked. Those cast shadows are the clearest proof that this is light crossing a real room and not a mural, and they must be plainly visible. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the top corners of the vault, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room. Three or four thin volumetric shafts of coloured light hang visibly in the air of the room so the beam itself is seen in the space, but they are narrow and crisply defined and they do NOT fog, veil or soften any part of the picture. EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP. Each spire and arched window of the gold city, each carriage window of the train, each antler and harness bell on each reindeer, each arm of each snowflake, each ribbon and bow on each floating gift and each ring of the great spiral has a hard, crisp, precisely drawn edge. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 4 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The light-oak floorboards, the large woven rug, the bed and its covers, the chair beside it and the little Christmas tree carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no city, no train, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. CHANGE 5 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no stool, no side table, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain knee wall and floor.

````text
Wholesome family Christmas scene, a child at home with his family at Christmas.

SUMMARY: a girl bounces on her bed in a vaulted Christmas bedroom at night while a projected Christmas night-sky picture is PLAYING across the board-and-batten gable wall behind the bed and both sloping drywall ceiling planes above it, thrown there by a projector standing on the floor. 5 seconds, one continuous handheld phone take, vertical.

GLOBAL STYLE: crisp real iPhone footage at night, handheld, real-time speed. Sharp and in focus from the first frame to the last, at least as sharp as the start image. No blur, no haze, no soft focus, no washed-out exposure, no colour shift.

ASSET ROLES: @Image 1 is frame 0.0s and controls the vaulted bedroom, its two smooth warm-white drywall ceiling slopes, the dark-walnut ridge beam and the dark-walnut rafter beams running up each slope, the dormer recess cut into the left slope, the low white knee walls, the white board-and-batten gable wall behind the bed with its picture-rail shelf and small high window, the bed with its rust-red wool quilt and cream linen pillows, the wide oatmeal upholstered headboard, the small lit Christmas tree, the large pale woven rug, the window with its garland, the stocking, THE GIRL and the colour.

SUBJECTS:
THE GIRL - exactly one child, about seven years old with shoulder-length wavy auburn hair, in a warm rust-red and cream nordic knitted Christmas jumper with a row of cream snowflakes across the yoke, charcoal knitted lounge trousers and thick oatmeal knitted socks, standing on the bed with her arms thrown wide. No second child, no adult, no pet.
THE PROJECTOR - a single Christmas projector switched on and running on the bedroom floor, out of shot. One lens throws one wide cone of light across the gable wall and both whole drywall ceiling slopes. Everything glowing on the drywall is that one cone landing.
THE PICTURE - the animated scene that cone is playing: a deep snow-laden pine forest running low along the walls, an ornate fairy-tale Christmas city built of lit gold rising out of the pines on the left with tier upon tier of spires and ribbed onion domes, a long lit steam train winding out of the pines on the right with a bright plume of steam and sparks trailing back over its glowing carriages, wide aurora ribbons in deep crimson and ruby red with warm gold and emerald green threaded between them sweeping up the walls and over both ceiling slopes, one colossal spiral whirlpool of light filling the vault with its centre on the ridge beam, Santa's sleigh drawn by eight flying reindeer with a long tapering trail sweeping out of the mouth of that spiral, a big pale blue full moon glowing high on the gable wall with the tiny black silhouette of a second sleigh crossing its face, floating wrapped Christmas gifts drifting at five or six different depths, a dense field of stars of many sizes, long-tailed shooting stars, and a heavy fall of large soft glowing six-point snowflakes and tiny points of light.

HOW THE PICTURE BEHAVES - THIS IS THE ENTIRE POINT OF THE CLIP. In every previous attempt the picture sat dead still like printed wallpaper and the clip was worthless. THE PICTURE IS A FILM PLAYING ON A CINEMA SCREEN: the screen is the drywall and holds perfectly still, while everything inside the picture is in constant motion for all five seconds. The start image is one paused frame of that film; at 0.0s it un-pauses. Matching @Image 1 applies to the bedroom, the bed, THE GIRL and the colours, not to where any element of the picture sits.
THE PICTURE AS A WHOLE IS ANCHORED. It does not rotate, spin, swing, sweep sideways or slide across the room as one sheet. Its outer boundary stays where it is. The motion lives INSIDE it, element by element:
- THE STEAM - the long lit steam train winding out of the pines is working hard and never stops pouring steam. The plume boils out of the funnel, leans back over the carriage roofs, rises about one carriage-height every second, widening and thinning as it climbs and fading out before it reaches the aurora, while a fresh plume is already forming at the funnel behind it. The plume is never a fixed painted shape.
- THE RISING SPARKS - a slow steady rise of glowing gold sparks lifts off the locomotive and climbs the full height of the wall to join the star field, drifting slightly sideways as it goes, new sparks leaving the funnel continuously so the column never empties.
- THE AURORA - each crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbon ripples ALONG ITS OWN LENGTH, a slow travelling wave running from one end of the ribbon to the other, about a third of the ribbon's length every second, the ribbon swelling brighter and thinner as the wave passes and its lower fringe combing and re-forming. Each ribbon's overall arc stays where it is on the drywall; the wave inside it travels. Every ribbon on the ceiling is doing this at the same time, and the ribbons that feed into the mouth of the great spiral keep streaming inward along their whole length.
- THE SLEIGH AND THE REINDEER - they are FLYING. The team travels steadily across the ceiling away from the mouth of the great spiral where it sits in the start image, out toward the far side of the ceiling, covering about a fifth of the ceiling's width every second, so by 5.0s it has crossed most of the way. The reindeer's legs gallop, their heads nod, the sleigh rocks gently behind them, the long tapering trail streams out behind, and the whole team rises and dips a little as it goes.
- THE GREAT SPIRAL - the colossal whirlpool of light filling the vault is WINDING and never still. All seven concentric rings turn steadily about the brilliant white-gold core sitting on the ridge beam, each ring creeping inward and tightening as it turns, completing roughly a sixth of a full turn every second, the countless tiny points of light inside them streaming along the curve like a slow river of stars circling a drain. Its outer boundary stays exactly where it is on the ceiling; only the rings inside it wind.
- THE FLOATING GIFTS - each glowing wrapped gift DRIFTS through the scene at its own depth and its own speed, crossing about a tenth of the frame every second, and every one of them turns slowly end over end as it goes so its ribbon cross and big soft bow swing round into view and away again.
- THE SNOWFLAKES AND THE POINTS OF LIGHT - each one drifts downward and slightly sideways about a quarter of the wall's height every second, the way snow falls past a window at night. Each turns slowly on its own centre as it drifts and breathes brighter and dimmer on its own timing. One that reaches the skirting board fades out there while a fresh one fades in high near the ridge beam, so the density holds even. Track this one so the fall is unmistakable: a large cream six-point flake that begins just above the headboard, falls steadily and drifts slightly right, passes the middle of the wall at 2.5s and fades out low near the skirting at 5.0s.
- THE SHOOTING STARS - the long-tailed shooting stars streak across, their tails thinning out behind them, and fade; fresh ones set off elsewhere.
- THE STAR FIELD - the small gold stars hold their places and twinkle, each brightening and dimming on its own timing, never all together.
- THE MOON - the pale blue moon is the ONLY element that holds its position. It stays exactly where it is high on the gable wall for the whole clip and only brightens and dims very slightly, while the tiny black silhouette of the second sleigh crawls steadily on across its face. POSITIVE LOCKS: real-time motion at normal speed - a real girl jumping at real speed and landing with real weight. Exactly one child. One continuous take with no cut. THE PICTURE - the animated scene that cone is playing: a deep snow-laden pine forest running low along the walls, an ornate fairy-tale Christmas city built of lit gold rising out of the pines on the left with tier upon tier of spires and ribbed onion domes, a long lit steam train winding out of the pines on the right with a bright plume of steam and sparks trailing back over its glowing carriages, wide aurora ribbons in deep crimson and ruby red with warm gold and emerald green threaded between them sweeping up the walls and over both ceiling slopes, one colossal spiral whirlpool of light filling the vault with its centre sitting on the ridge beam, Santa's sleigh drawn by eight flying reindeer with a long tapering trail sweeping out of the mouth of that spiral, a big pale blue full moon glowing high on one side wall with the tiny black silhouette of a second sleigh crossing its face, floating wrapped Christmas gifts drifting at five or six different depths, a dense field of stars of many sizes, long-tailed shooting stars, and a heavy fall of large soft glowing six-point snowflakes and tiny points of light.

The plume boils out of the funnel, leans back over the carriage roofs, rises about one carriage-height every second, widening and thinning as it climbs and fading out before it reaches the aurora, while a fresh plume is already forming at the funnel behind it. The plume is never a fixed painted shape.
- THE RISING SPARKS - a slow steady rise of glowing gold sparks lifts off the locomotive and climbs the full height of the wall to join the star field, drifting slightly sideways as it goes, new sparks leaving the funnel continuously so the column never empties.
- THE AURORA - each crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbon ripples ALONG ITS OWN LENGTH, a slow travelling wave running from one end of the ribbon to the other, about a third of the ribbon's length every second, the ribbon swelling brighter and thinner as the wave passes and its lower fringe combing and re-forming. Each ribbon's overall arc stays where it is on the drywall; the wave inside it travels. Every ribbon on the wall and on the ceiling is doing this at the same time, and the ribbons that feed into the mouth of the great spiral keep streaming inward along their whole length.
- THE SLEIGH AND THE REINDEER - they are FLYING. The team travels steadily across the ceiling away from the mouth of the great spiral where it sits in the start image, covering about a fifth of the ceiling's width every second, so by 4.0s it has crossed most of the way. The reindeer's legs gallop, their heads nod, the sleigh rocks gently behind them, the long tapering trail streams out behind, and the whole team rises and dips a little as it goes. All seven concentric rings turn steadily about the brilliant white-gold core sitting on the ridge beam, each ring creeping inward and tightening as it turns, completing roughly a sixth of a full turn every second, the countless tiny points of light inside them streaming along the curve like a slow river of stars circling a drain. One that reaches the floor line fades out there while a fresh one fades in high near the ridge beam, so the density holds even.
- THE SMALL TRAVELLERS - the floating wrapped Christmas gifts drift through the scene at their own depths, each one turning slowly end over end as it goes so its ribbon cross and big soft bow swing round into view and away again; the long-tailed shooting stars streak across, their tails thinning out behind them, and fade, with fresh ones setting off elsewhere.
- THE STAR FIELD - the small gold stars hold their places and twinkle, each brightening and dimming on its own timing, never all together.
- THE MOON - the pale blue moon is the ONLY element that holds its position. It stays exactly where it is on the side wall for the whole clip and only brightens and dims very slightly, while the tiny black silhouette of the second sleigh crawls steadily on across its face. Nothing else in the picture is allowed to hold still.

Whatever the flakes, the aurora and the stars on the wall are doing, the flakes, the aurora and the stars on the ceiling are doing at the same moment and at the same speed. Both children stay low in the frame and keep moving.

AUDIO: room tone, two children laughing and squealing, feet thumping on the sofa cushions, feet on a rug. (no BGM). No subtitles.

POSITIVE LOCKS: real-time motion at normal speed throughout - real children moving at real speed with real weight. Exactly two children, and their knitted jumpers read clearly. One continuous take with no cut. The camera makes one move per beat and no more. The picture's outer boundary holds still while every element inside it moves. Walls and ceiling always move together. THE OTHER CHILD stays on the sofa. The picture stays sharp to the last frame.

DO NOT INCLUDE: a frozen or still pattern; a locomotive with no moving steam plume; an aurora that sits still; a sleigh and reindeer parked in one place; a spiral whose rings do not wind; a floating gift that does not drift and turn; flakes that only twinkle without travelling; a still ceiling above moving walls; the whole picture rotating, spinning, sweeping or sliding as one sheet; slow motion; floating or weightless movement; any music; children standing still; a third child; an adult; the projector unit in shot; jellyfish, fish, coral or bubbles; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.

The great spiral is an AURORA WHIRLPOOL made of Christmas light with a sleigh flying out of its mouth, not a space nebula: it must NOT look like outer space, a galaxy, a wormhole, a tunnel or anything science-fiction, alien or dystopian - it is a storybook Christmas night sky with a lit gold Christmas city, a steam train, flying reindeer and falling gifts in it. Every element is a separate glowing shape sitting on the surface - nothing is painted, printed, stencilled or wallpapered, and nothing is a flat repeating texture. HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely. The white painted drywall, the white battens and the pale stone do NOT read as white anywhere inside the projection field - the light has swallowed their colour. The only things that survive through it are the surface's TEXTURE and its SHADOWS: the slight tooth of the drywall, the joints and edges, the mortar lines of the stone, the square corners of the beams and battens. That is the ten percent; the colour of the surface is not part of it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white. Contrast is extreme: the cores of the gold city, the train, the spiral and the stars are near-white hot, the mid-tones are deep saturated crimson and emerald, and the empty sky between them is almost black - never pale paint. AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on. Each of the two ceiling slopes is a separate plane at its own angle, and every shape lying on a slope is keystoned and skewed by THAT plane's perspective, so the same aurora band is stretched differently on the left slope than on the right. Every band, every ring of the spiral and every large shape that crosses the ridge visibly FOLDS at the ridge line, changing direction there with a clean kink, exactly like a picture projected across the inside of a tent. Where the light reaches the top of a side wall it breaks again at that corner and runs down the vertical plane at a different angle. The ridge beam and every rafter beam interrupt it: the face of each beam that points back toward the projector catches its own bright slice of the picture, offset and stepped from the piece on the drywall behind it, while the underside and the far side of every beam sit in hard unlit shadow, so the whole ceiling reads as a lit vault ribbed by dark beams and never as one flat painted mural. The white board-and-batten wainscot breaks it again, every raised batten catching its own bright vertical strip and throwing its own shadow so the picture ripples across the lower wall in a row of ribs. The stacked-stone chimney breast breaks it a third time, every stone catching the light on its own face and throwing its own small shadow, and the white loft railing breaks it a fourth, each square baluster catching a bright edge and casting a bar of shadow onto the wall behind it. The surface texture stays legible inside the light - the tooth of the drywall, the square edges of the beams and battens, the mortar joints of the stone - so it still reads as projected light on a real room and never as paint, wallpaper, a sticker or a printed panel. AND EVERY PIECE OF STANDING-PROUD TIMBER AND TRIM THROWS A REAL SHADOW: each chunky beam and each raised batten casts a hard-edged shadow of itself across the surface behind it, so a dark unlit band runs alongside every beam and every batten where the picture is blocked. Those cast shadows are the clearest proof that this is light crossing a real room and not a mural, and they must be plainly visible. Falloff is gentle and only in the extreme far corners; the picture stays strong and fully coloured right across both slopes and both walls. Three or four faint volumetric shafts of coloured light hang visibly in the air of the room, so the beam itself is seen in the space.

CHANGE 4 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The light-oak floorboards, the jute rug, the light-grey sectional sofa, the big ottoman and the tree carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no city, no train, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow.
